user
You are the second reviewer on a routine security code review of our own
nix-bitcoin fork: the NixOS modules and packaging we deploy on our own Bitcoin
and Lightning node. Two prior reviewers have already produced the findings
below against the exact commit checked out in the current directory. Your job
is quality control on their work, as the maintainer's independent check:

For EACH finding, open the cited file at the cited location and decide:
- verdict: "confirmed" (the defect is real as described), "false-positive"
  (the code does not do what the finding claims, or an existing control
  already prevents it — name the control), or "needs-info" (cannot be decided
  from the tree; say what is missing).
- severity: your own call (critical/high/medium/low/info), which may differ
  from the reviewer's.
- reasoning: one to three sentences grounded in the code you read (quote the
  line or construct that decides it).
- fix_ok: whether the reviewer's recommended fix is correct and complete
  (true/false) and, if false, what is wrong with it.

Then, only for the files the findings touch, note up to three defects the
reviewers missed or misread that a maintainer should know about ("missed").
Do not pad: if there is nothing, say so.

Read-only tools only; do not modify the tree. Be terse and concrete.

End your answer with exactly one fenced ```json block of this shape:
{"reviews":[{"id":"<finding id or title>","verdict":"confirmed|false-positive|needs-info","severity":"...","reasoning":"...","fix_ok":true,"fix_note":""}],
 "missed":[{"file":"...","line":0,"severity":"...","title":"...","description":"..."}],
 "summary":"two or three sentences"}
followed by nothing else.

    "description": "nbxplorer and btcpayserver connect via the /run/postgresql unix socket. Unix-domain sockets are not isolated by netns-isolation, so for these two services the postgres HBA is the actual service-isolation boundary. The module sets no authentication policy and inherits whatever the pinned nixpkgs postgresql module defaults to for local socket auth. If that default is (or becomes) weaker than peer auth for the relevant roles, any local compromised service user can connect as btcpayserver/nbxplorer and fully administer btcpaydb and the nbxplorer DB (invoice/store data, derived-address metadata).",

    "recommendation": "Set services.postgresql.authentication explicitly in the module (peer for local unix-socket connections, or scram for anything else) so the boundary does not track nixpkgs defaults across weekly pin moves; verify the generated pg_hba.conf in the VM test."

   182	
   183	    wallet-unlock-password-file=${secretsDir}/lnd-wallet-password
   184	
   185	    ${cfg.extraConfig}
   186	  '';
   187	
   188	  zmqHandleSpecialAddress = builtins.replaceStrings [ "0.0.0.0" "[::]" ] [ "127.0.0.1" "[::1]" ];
   189	in {
   190	
   191	  inherit options;
   192	
   193	  config = mkIf cfg.enable {
   194	    services.bitcoind = {
   195	      enable = true;
   196	
   197	      # Increase rpc thread count due to reports that lightning implementations fail
   198	      # under high bitcoind rpc load
   199	      rpc.threads = 16;
   200	
   201	      zmqpubrawblock = mkDefault "tcp://${bitcoindRpcAddress}:28332";
   202	      zmqpubrawtx = mkDefault "tcp://${bitcoindRpcAddress}:28333";
   203	    };
   204	
   205	    environment.systemPackages = [ cfg.package (hiPrio cfg.cli) ];
   206	
   207	    systemd.tmpfiles.rules = [
   208	      "d '${cfg.dataDir}' 0770 ${cfg.user} ${cfg.group} - -"
   209	    ];
   210	
   211	    services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = mkIf (cfg.rpcAddress != "127.0.0.1") [ "${cfg.rpcAddress}" ];
   212	
   213	    systemd.services.lnd = {
   214	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   215	      # `wants`, not `requires`: lnd is built to tolerate its chain backend
   216	      # going away and reconnects on its own. A hard `requires` meant every
   217	      # bitcoind restart (e.g. any deploy that touches bitcoind config) STOPPED
   218	      # lnd, and `nixos-rebuild switch` did not restart it — leaving lnd (and
   219	      # Lightning) down until a manual start. `after` still orders lnd behind
   220	      # bitcoind at boot; `wants` pulls bitcoind in without the cascade-stop.
   221	      wants = [ "bitcoind.service" ];
   222	      after = [ "bitcoind.service" "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   223	      preStart = ''
   224	        install -m600 ${configFile} '${cfg.dataDir}/lnd.conf'
   225	        {
   226	          echo "bitcoind.rpcpass=$(cat ${secretsDir}/bitcoin-rpcpassword-public)"
   227	          ${optionalString (cfg.getPublicAddressCmd != "") ''
   228	            echo "externalip=$(${cfg.getPublicAddressCmd})"
   229	          ''}
   230	        } >> '${cfg.dataDir}/lnd.conf'
   231	
   232	        if [[ ! -f ${networkDir}/wallet.db ]]; then
   233	          seed='${cfg.dataDir}/lnd-seed-mnemonic'
   234	
   235	          if [[ ! -f "$seed" ]]; then
   236	            echo "Create lnd seed"
   237	            (umask u=r,go=; ${lndinit} gen-seed > "$seed")
   238	          fi
   239	
   240	          echo "Create lnd wallet"
   241	          ${lndinit} -v init-wallet \
   242	            --file.seed="$seed" \
   243	            --file.wallet-password='${secretsDir}/lnd-wallet-password' \
   244	            --init-file.output-wallet-dir='${cfg.networkDir}'
   245	        fi
   246	      '';
   247	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   248	        Type = "notify";
   249	        RuntimeDirectory = "lnd"; # Only used to store custom macaroons
   250	        RuntimeDirectoryMode = "711";
   251	        ExecStart = "${cfg.package}/bin/lnd --configfile='${cfg.dataDir}/lnd.conf'";
   252	        User = cfg.user;
   253	        TimeoutSec = "15min";
   254	        Restart = "on-failure";
   255	        RestartSec = "10s";
   256	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.dataDir ];
   257	        ExecStartPost = let
   258	          curl = "${pkgs.curl}/bin/curl -fsS --cacert ${cfg.certPath}";
   259	          restUrl = "https://${nbLib.addressWithPort cfg.restAddress cfg.restPort}/v1";
   260	          # Setting macaroon permissions for other users needs root permissions
   261	          # The admin macaroon is passed to curl via a fd because argv is
   262	          # world-readable through /proc/<pid>/cmdline
   263	          script = nbLib.rootScript "lnd-create-macaroons" ''
   264	            umask ug=r,o=
   265	            # $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY (/run/lnd) is writable by the lnd service user,
   266	            # so this root script must never write/chmod/chown through a path the
   267	            # lnd process could tamper with (else a compromised lnd escalates to
   268	            # arbitrary root file overwrite/chown). Staging in $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY
   269	            # is NOT enough — lnd owns that directory and can unlink our temp file
   270	            # and swap in a symlink before we write to it. Stage instead in a
   271	            # ROOT-ONLY directory (mode 0700, owned by root, that lnd cannot enter
   272	            # or replace) on the SAME tmpfs as /run/lnd, so the final move is a
   273	            # rename(2): atomic, and it replaces the destination without following
   274	            # a symlink lnd may have planted there.
   275	            staging=$(${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mktemp -d "/run/lnd-macaroons.XXXXXX")
   276	            trap '${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/rm -rf "$staging"' EXIT
   277	            ${lib.concatMapStrings (macaroon: ''
   278	              echo "Create custom macaroon ${macaroon}"
   279	              macaroonTmp="$staging/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   280	              # Read admin.macaroon AS the lnd user, never as root: it lives in
   281	              # lnd's own 0770 datadir, so a compromised lnd could plant a
   282	              # symlink there and have this root script dereference it to
   283	              # exfiltrate an arbitrary root-only file (backup passphrase, wg
   284	              # key, host keys) via the REST call below. Reading as the lnd user
   285	              # fails closed on such a symlink — lnd cannot read what lnd cannot
   286	              # read — and grants no access lnd does not already have. (The write
   287	              # path above is already symlink-hardened; this closes the read.)
   288	              adminMacaroonHex=$(${pkgs.util-linux}/bin/runuser -u ${cfg.user} -- ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -ps -u -c 99999 '${networkDir}/admin.macaroon')
   289	              # The response body is lnd-controlled. A macaroon is a few hundred
   290	              # bytes, so cap what this root script will buffer and write to the
   291	              # /run tmpfs at 64 KiB, and fail (jq -e) rather than write garbage
   292	              # when the reply carries no `.macaroon` (audit 2026-08-30, low).
   293	              ${curl} \
   294	                -H @<(printf 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: %s\n' "$adminMacaroonHex") \
   295	                -X POST \
   296	                -d '{"permissions":[${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.permissions}]}' \
   297	                ${restUrl}/macaroon |\
   298	                ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/head -c 65536 |\
   299	                ${pkgs.jq}/bin/jq -ce '.macaroon | strings' | ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -p -r > "$macaroonTmp"
   300	              chown ${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.user}: "$macaroonTmp"
   301	              ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mv -f "$macaroonTmp" "$RUNTIME_DIRECTORY/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   302	            '') (attrNames cfg.macaroons)}
   303	          '';
   304	        in [
   305	          script
   306	        ];
   307	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce;
   308	    };
   309	
   310	    users.users.${cfg.user} = {
   311	      isSystemUser = true;
   312	      group = cfg.group;
   313	      extraGroups = [ "bitcoinrpc-public" ];
   314	      home = cfg.dataDir; # lnd creates .lnd dir in HOME
   315	    };
   316	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   317	    nix-bitcoin.operator = {
   318	      groups = [ cfg.group ];
   319	      allowRunAsUsers = [ cfg.user ];
   320	    };
   321	
   322	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   323	      lnd-wallet-password.user = cfg.user;
   324	      lnd-key.user = cfg.user;
   325	      lnd-cert.user = cfg.user;
   326	      lnd-cert.permissions = "444"; # world readable
   327	    };
   328	    # Advantages of manually pre-generating certs:
   329	    # - Reduces dynamic state
   330	    # - Enables deployment of a mesh of server plus client nodes with predefined certs
   331	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.lnd = ''
   332	      makePasswordSecret lnd-wallet-password
   333	      makeCert lnd '${nbLib.mkCertExtraAltNames cfg.certificate}'
   334	    '';
   335	  };
   336	}
     1	{ config, lib, pkgs, ... }:

     4	let
     5	  options.nix-bitcoin.operator = {

     7	      type = types.bool;
     8	      default = false;

    13	        When using nix-bitcoin as part of a larger system config, it makes sense

     1	{ config, lib, pkgs, ... }:
     2	
     3	with lib;
     4	let
     5	  options.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation = {
     6	    enable = mkEnableOption "netns isolation";
     7	
     8	    addressblock = mkOption {
     9	      type = types.ints.u8;
    10	      default = 1;
    11	      description = ''
    12	        The address block N in 169.254.N.0/24, used as the prefix for netns addresses.
    13	      '';
    14	    };
    15	
    16	    services = mkOption {
    17	      default = {};
    18	      type = types.attrsOf (types.submodule {
    19	        options = {
    20	          id = mkOption {
    21	            # TODO: Assert uniqueness
    22	            type = types.ints.between 11 255;
    23	            description = ''
    24	              id for the netns, used for the IP address host part and
    25	              for naming the interfaces. Must be unique. Must be greater than 10.
    26	            '';
    27	          };
    28	          connections = mkOption {
    29	            type = with types; listOf str;
    30	            default = [];
    31	          };
    32	        };
    33	      });
    34	    };
    35	
    36	    allowedUser = mkOption {
    37	      type = types.str;
    38	      description = ''
    39	        User that is allowed to execute commands in the service network namespaces.
    40	      '';
    41	      default = config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name;
    42	    };
    43	
    44	    netns = mkOption {
    45	      readOnly = true;
    46	      default = netns;
    47	      description = "Exposes netns parameters.";
    48	    };
    49	
    50	    bridgeIp = mkOption {
    51	      readOnly = true;
    52	      default = bridgeIp;
    53	      description = "IP of the netns bridge interface.";
    54	    };
    55	  };
    56	
    57	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation;
    58	
    59	  netns = builtins.mapAttrs (n: v: {
    60	    inherit (v) id;
    61	    address = "169.254.${toString cfg.addressblock}.${toString v.id}";
    62	    availableNetns = availableNetns.${n};
    63	    netnsName = "nb-${n}";
    64	  }) enabledServices;
    65	
    66	  # Symmetric netns connection matrix
    67	  # if clightning.connections = [ "bitcoind" ]; then
    68	  #   availableNetns.bitcoind = [ "clighting" ];
    69	  #   and
    70	  #   availableNetns.clighting = [ "bitcoind" ];
    71	  #
    72	  # TODO-EXTERNAL:
    73	  # Although negligible for our purposes, this calculation's runtime
    74	  # is in the order of (number of connections * number of services),
    75	  # because attrsets and lists are fully copied on each update with '//' or '++'.
    76	  # This can only be improved with an update in the nix language.
    77	  #
    78	  availableNetns = let
    79	    # base = { clightning = [ "bitcoind" ]; ... }
    80	    base = builtins.mapAttrs (n: v:
    81	      builtins.filter isEnabled v.connections
    82	    ) enabledServices;
    83	  in
    84	    foldl (xs: s1:
    85	      foldl (xs: s2:
    86	        xs // { "${s2}" = xs.${s2} ++ [ s1 ]; }
    87	      ) xs cfg.services.${s1}.connections
    88	    ) base (builtins.attrNames base);
    89	
    90	  enabledServices = filterAttrs (n: v: isEnabled n) cfg.services;
    91	  isEnabled = x: config.services.${x}.enable;
    92	
    93	  ip = "${pkgs.iproute2}/bin/ip";
    94	  iptables = "${config.networking.firewall.package}/bin/iptables";
    95	
    96	  bridgeIp = "169.254.${toString cfg.addressblock}.10";
    97	in {
    98	  inherit options;
    99	
   100	  config = mkIf cfg.enable (mkMerge [
   101	
   102	  # Base infrastructure
   103	  {
   104	    networking.dhcpcd.denyInterfaces = [ "nb-br" "nb-veth*" ];
   105	    services.tor.client.socksListenAddress = {
   106	      addr = bridgeIp;
   107	      # Default NixOS values. These must be repeated when redefining this option.
   108	      port = 9050;
   109	      IsolateDestAddr = true;
   110	    };
   111	    services.i2pd.proto.sam.address = bridgeIp;
   112	    networking.firewall.interfaces.nb-br.allowedTCPPorts = [
   113	      config.services.tor.client.socksListenAddress.port
   114	      config.services.i2pd.proto.sam.port
   115	    ];
   116	    boot.kernel.sysctl."net.ipv4.ip_forward" = true;
   117	
   118	    security.wrappers.netns-exec = {
   119	      source = config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.netns-exec;
   120	      capabilities = "cap_sys_admin=ep";
   121	      owner = cfg.allowedUser;
   122	      # Don't authorize the group of `owner`. For normal users, this group is
   123	      # `users`, which is shared by all normal users.
   124	      group = "root";
   125	      permissions = "500";
   126	    };
   127	
   128	    systemd.services = {
   129	      # Due to a NixOS bug we can't currently use option `networking.bridges` to
   130	      # setup the bridge while `networking.useDHCP` is enabled.
   131	      nb-netns-bridge = {
   132	        description = "nix-bitcoin netns bridge";
   133	        wantedBy = [ "network-setup.service" ];
   134	        partOf = [ "network-setup.service" ];
   135	        before = [ "network-setup.service" ];
   136	        after = [ "network-pre.target" ];
   137	        serviceConfig = {
   138	          Type = "oneshot";
   139	          RemainAfterExit = true;
   140	        };
   141	        script = ''
   142	          ${ip} link add name nb-br type bridge
   143	          ${ip} link set nb-br up
   144	          ${ip} addr add ${bridgeIp}/24 brd + dev nb-br
   145	          ${iptables} -w -t nat -A POSTROUTING -s 169.254.${toString cfg.addressblock}.0/24 -j MASQUERADE
   146	        '';
   147	        preStop = ''
   148	          ${iptables} -w -t nat -D POSTROUTING -s 169.254.${toString cfg.addressblock}.0/24 -j MASQUERADE
   149	          ${ip} link del nb-br
   150	        '';
   151	      };
   152	
   153	    } //
   154	    (let
   155	      makeNetnsServices = n: v: let
   156	        veth = "nb-veth-${toString v.id}";
   157	        peer = "nb-veth-br-${toString v.id}";
   158	        inherit (v) netnsName;
   159	        nsenter = "${pkgs.util-linux}/bin/nsenter";
   160	        allowedNetnsAddresses = map (available: netns.${available}.address) v.availableNetns;
   161	        allowedAddresses = concatStringsSep ","
   162	          ([ "127.0.0.1,${bridgeIp},${v.address}" ] ++ allowedNetnsAddresses);
   163	
   164	        setup = ''
   165	          ${ip} netns add ${netnsName}
   166	          ${ip} link add ${veth} type veth peer name ${peer}
   167	          ${ip} link set ${veth} netns ${netnsName}
   168	          # The peer link is never used directly, so don't auto-assign an IPv6 address
   169	          echo 1 > /proc/sys/net/ipv6/conf/${peer}/disable_ipv6
   170	          ${ip} link set ${peer} up
   171	          ${ip} link set ${peer} master nb-br
   172	          exec ${nsenter} --net=/run/netns/${netnsName} ${script "in-netns" setupInNetns}
   173	        '';
   174	
   175	        setupInNetns = ''
   176	          ${ip} link set lo up
   177	          ${ip} addr add ${v.address}/24 dev ${veth}
   178	          ${ip} link set ${veth} up
   179	          ${ip} route add default via ${bridgeIp}
   180	
   181	          ${iptables} -w -P INPUT DROP
   182	          # allow return traffic to outgoing connections initiated by the service itself
   183	          ${iptables} -w -A INPUT -m conntrack --ctstate ESTABLISHED -j ACCEPT
   184	          ${iptables} -w -A INPUT -s ${allowedAddresses} -j ACCEPT
   185	        '' + optionalString (config.services.${n}.tor.enforce or false) ''
   186	          ${iptables} -w -P OUTPUT DROP
   187	          ${iptables} -w -A OUTPUT -d ${allowedAddresses} -j ACCEPT
   188	        '';
   189	        script = name: src: pkgs.writers.writeDash name ''
   190	          set -e
   191	          ${src}
   192	        '';
   193	      in {
   194	        "${n}".serviceConfig.NetworkNamespacePath = "/var/run/netns/${netnsName}";
   195	
   196	        "netns-${n}" = rec {
   197	          requires = [ "nb-netns-bridge.service" ];
   198	          after = [ "nb-netns-bridge.service" ];
   199	          requiredBy = [ "${n}.service" ];
   200	          before = requiredBy;
   201	          serviceConfig = {
   202	            Type = "oneshot";
   203	            RemainAfterExit = true;
   204	            ExecStart = script "setup" setup;
   205	          };
   206	          # Link deletion is implicit in netns deletion, but it sometimes only happens
   207	          # after `netns delete` finishes. Add an extra `link del` to ensure that
   208	          # the link is deleted before the service stops, which is needed for service
   209	          # restart to succeed.
   210	          preStop = ''
   211	            ${ip} netns delete ${netnsName}
   212	            ${ip} link del ${peer} 2> /dev/null || true
   213	          '';
   214	
   215	        };
   216	      };
   217	    in
   218	      foldl (services: n:
   219	        services // (makeNetnsServices n netns.${n})
   220	      ) {} (builtins.attrNames netns)
   221	    );
   222	  }
   223	
   224	  # Service-specific config
   225	  {
   226	    nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.services = {
   227	      # ids of services removed in the fork trim stay reserved:
   228	      # 13 clightning, 15 liquidd, 21 nginx, 22 lightning-loop, 25 joinmarket,
   229	      # 26 joinmarket-ob-watcher, 27 lightning-pool, 28 charge-lnd, 29 rtl,
   230	      # 30 clightning-rest, 31 fulcrum, 32 mempool.
   231	      bitcoind = {
   232	        id = 12;
   233	      };
   234	      lnd = {
   235	        id = 14;
   236	        connections = [ "bitcoind" ];
   237	      };
   238	      electrs = {
   239	        id = 16;
   240	        connections = [ "bitcoind" ];
   241	      };
   242	      nbxplorer = {
   243	        id = 23;
   244	        connections = [ "bitcoind" ];
   245	      };
   246	      btcpayserver = {
   247	        id = 24;
   248	        connections = [ "nbxplorer" ]
   249	                      ++ optional (config.services.btcpayserver.lightningBackend == "lnd") "lnd";
   250	      };
   251	    };
   252	
   253	    services.bitcoind = {
   254	      address = netns.bitcoind.address;
   255	      rpc.address = netns.bitcoind.address;
   256	      rpc.allowip = [
   257	        bridgeIp # For operator user
   258	        netns.bitcoind.address
   259	      ] ++ map (n: netns.${n}.address) netns.bitcoind.availableNetns;
   260	    };
   261	
   262	    services.lnd = {
   263	      address = netns.lnd.address;
   264	      rpcAddress = netns.lnd.address;
   265	      restAddress = netns.lnd.address;
   266	    };
   267	
   268	    services.electrs.address = netns.electrs.address;
   269	
   270	    services.nbxplorer.address = netns.nbxplorer.address;
   271	    services.btcpayserver.address = netns.btcpayserver.address;
   272	  }
   273	  ]);
   274	}

     3	# Create a WireGuard server with a single peer.
     4	# Private/public keys are created via the secrets system.
     5	# Add helper binaries `nix-bitcoin-wg-connect` and optionally `lndconnect-wg`.
     6	
     7	# See ../../docs/services.md ("Use Zeus (mobile lightning wallet) via WireGuard")
     8	# for usage instructions.
     9	
    10	# This is a rather opinionated implementation that lacks the flexibility offered by
    11	# other nix-bitcoin modules, so ship this as a `preset`.
    12	# Some users will prefer to use `lndconnect` with their existing WireGuard or Tailscale setup.
    13	
    14	with lib;
    15	let
    16	  options.nix-bitcoin.wireguard = {
    17	    subnet = mkOption {
    18	      type = types.str;
    19	      default = "10.10.0";
    20	      description = "The /24 subnet of the wireguard network.";
    21	    };
    22	    restrictPeer = mkOption {
    23	      type = types.bool;
    24	      default = true;
    25	      description = ''
    26	        Prevent the peer from connecting to any addresses except for the WireGuard server address.
    27	      '';
    28	    };
    29	  };
    30	
    31	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.wireguard;
    32	  wgSubnet = cfg.subnet;
    33	  inherit (config.networking.wireguard.interfaces) wg-nb;
    34	  inherit (config.services) lnd;
    35	
    36	  lndconnect = lnd.enable && lnd.lndconnect.enable;
    37	
    38	  serverAddress = "${wgSubnet}.1";
    39	  peerAddress = "${wgSubnet}.2";
    40	
    41	  secretsDir = config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir;
    42	
    43	  wgConnectUser = if config.nix-bitcoin.operator.enable
    44	                  then config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name
    45	                  else "root";
    46	
    47	  # A script that prints a QR code to connect a peer to the server.
    48	  # The QR code encodes a wg-quick config that can be imported by the wireguard
    49	  # mobile app.
    50	  wgConnect = pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "nix-bitcoin-wg-connect" ''
    51	    set -euo pipefail
    52	    text=
    53	    host=
    54	    for arg in "$@"; do
    55	      case $arg in
    56	        --text)
    57	          text=1
    58	          ;;
    59	        *)
    60	          host=$arg
    61	          ;;
    62	      esac
    63	    done
    64	
    65	    if [[ ! $host ]]; then
    66	      # Use lndconnect to fetch the external ip.
    67	      # This internally uses https://github.com/GlenDC/go-external-ip, which
    68	      # queries a set of external ip providers.
    69	      host=$(
    70	        ${getExe config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.lndconnect} --url --nocert \
    71	          --configfile=/dev/null --adminmacaroonpath=/dev/null \
    72	          | sed -nE 's|.*?/(.*?):.*|\1|p'
    73	      )
    74	    fi
    75	
    76	    config="[Interface]
    77	    PrivateKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-peer-private-key)
    78	    Address = ${peerAddress}/24
    79	
    80	    [Peer]
    81	    PublicKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-server-public-key)
    82	    AllowedIPs = ${wgSubnet}.0/24
    83	    Endpoint = $host:${toString wg-nb.listenPort}
    84	    PersistentKeepalive = 25
    85	    "
    86	
    87	    if [[ $text ]]; then
    88	      echo "$config"
    89	    else
    90	      echo "$config" | ${getExe pkgs.qrencode} -t UTF8 -o -
    91	    fi
    92	  '';
    93	in {
    94	  inherit options;
    95	
    96	  config = {
    97	    assertions = [
    98	      {
    99	        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
   100	        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
   101	        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
   102	      }
   103	    ];
   104	
   105	    networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb = {
   106	      ips = [ "${serverAddress}/24" ];
   107	      listenPort = mkDefault 51820;
   108	      privateKeyFile = "${secretsDir}/wg-server-private-key";
   109	      allowedIPsAsRoutes = false;
   110	      peers = [
   111	        {
   112	          # To use the actual public key from the secrets file, use dummy pubkey
   113	          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
   114	          # at peer service runtime.
   115	          publicKey = "peer0";
   116	          allowedIPs = [ "${peerAddress}/32" ];
   117	        }
   118	      ];
   119	    };
   120	
   121	    systemd.services = {
   122	      wireguard-wg-nb = rec {
   123	        wants = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   124	        after = wants;
   125	      };
   126	
   127	      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
   128	      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
   129	      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
   130	      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
   131	        script = mkForce ''
   132	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" allowed-ips ${peerAddress}/32
   133	        '';
   134	        postStop = mkForce ''
   135	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" remove || true
   136	        '';
   137	      };
   138	    };
   139	
   140	    environment.systemPackages = [
   141	      wgConnect
   142	    ] ++ (optional lndconnect
   143	      (pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "lndconnect-wg" ''
   144	        exec lndconnect --host "${serverAddress}" --nocert "$@"
   145	      '')
   146	    );
   147	
   148	    networking.firewall = let
   149	      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
   150	    in {
   151	      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
   152	
   153	      extraCommands =
   154	        optionalString lndconnect ''
   155	          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
   156	          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
   157	          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
   158	          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
   159	          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
   160	          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
   161	        ''
   162	        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
   163	          iptables -w -A nixos-fw ${restrictPeerRule}
   164	          iptables -w -A FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule}
   165	        '';
   166	
   167	      extraStopCommands =
   168	        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
   169	        # the NixOS firewall service.
   170	        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
   171	          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
   172	        '';
   173	    };
   174	
   175	    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
   176	    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
   177	    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
   178	    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
   179	    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
   180	    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
   181	    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
   182	    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
   183	    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
   184	    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
   185	    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
   186	      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
   187	      tor.enforce = false;
   188	    };
   189	
   190	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   191	      wg-server-private-key = {};
   192	      wg-server-public-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   193	      wg-peer-private-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   194	      wg-peer-public-key = {};
   195	    };
   196	
   197	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.wireguard = let
   198	      wg = "${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg";
   199	    in ''
   200	      makeWireguardKey() {
   201	        local name=$1
   202	        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
   203	        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
   204	        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
   205	          ${wg} genkey > $priv
   206	        fi
   207	        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
   208	          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
   209	        fi
   210	      }
   211	      makeWireguardKey server
   212	      makeWireguardKey peer
   213	    '';
   214	  };
   215	}
     1	# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
     2	
     3	Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
     4	
     5	The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
     6	that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
     7	(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
     8	interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
     9	accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
    10	The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
    11	10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
    12	things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
    13	
    14	> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
    15	> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
    16	> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
    17	> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
    18	
    19	## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
    20	
    21	1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
    22	   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
    23	   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
    24	   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
    25	   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
    26	   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
    27	   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
    28	   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
    29	   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
    30	   auditor trade-off of its own).
    31	
    32	2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
    33	   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
    34	   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
    35	   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
    36	   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
    37	   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
    38	   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
    39	   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
    40	   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
    41	   must tolerate rotation.
    42	
    43	3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
    44	   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
    45	   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
    46	   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
    47	   the daemon is healthy.
    48	
    49	4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
    50	   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
    51	   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
    52	   wg-dependent.
    53	
    54	5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
    55	   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
    56	   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
    57	
    58	6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
    59	   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
    60	
    61	## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
    62	
    63	Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
    64	the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
    65	entries:
    66	
    67	```nix
    68	services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
    69	  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
    70	'';
    71	```
    72	
    73	All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
    74	wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
    75	targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
    76	dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
    77	
    78	- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
    79	  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
    80	  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
    81	- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
    82	  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
    83	  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
    84	  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
    85	  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
    86	
    87	## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
    88	
    89	- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
    90	- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
    91	      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
    92	      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
    93	      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
    94	      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
    95	      connects.
    96	- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
    97	- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
    98	      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
    99	      existing clients.
   100	- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
   101	      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
   102	      items 1–6.

    36	      SystemCallFilter = [ "@system-service" "~add_key kcmp keyctl mbind move_pages name_to_handle_at personality process_vm_readv process_vm_writev request_key setns unshare userfaultfd" ];

    49	  };

    52	  allowLocalIPAddresses = {
    53	    IPAddressAllow = [

    55	      "::1/128"
    56	      "169.254.0.0/16"
    57	    ];
    58	  };
    59	  allowAllIPAddresses = { IPAddressAllow = "any"; };
    60	  allowTor = self.allowLocalIPAddresses;
    61	  allowedIPAddresses = onlyLocal:
    62	    if onlyLocal
    63	    then self.allowLocalIPAddresses
    64	    else self.allowAllIPAddresses;

    71	    };
    72	    enforce = mkOption {
    73	      type = types.bool;
    74	      default = false;
    75	      description = ''
    76	        Whether to enforce Tor on this service by only allowing connections
    77	        from and to localhost and link-local addresses.
    78	      '';
    79	    };
    80	  };
    81	
    82	  script = name: src: pkgs.writers.writeBash name ''
    83	    set -eo pipefail
    84	    ${src}
    85	  '';
    86	
    87	  # Used for ExecStart*
    88	  rootScript = name: src: "+${self.script name src}";
    89	
    90	  mkOnionService = map: {
    91	    map = [ map ];
    92	    version = 3;
    93	  };
    94	
    95	  # Convert a bind address, which may be a special INADDR_ANY address,
    96	  # to an actual IP address
    97	  address = addr:
    98	    if addr == "0.0.0.0" then
    99	      "127.0.0.1"
   100	    else if addr == "::" then
   101	      "::1"
   102	    else

   106

   112	      (map (ip: "IP:${ip}") cert.extraIPs)

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# cve-scan.sh — scan the fork's shipped service closure for known CVEs.
     3	#
     4	# We ship NixOS MODULES, not a flat package set, so the honest scan target is a
     5	# built node's RUNTIME closure. test/ci/scan-node.nix builds a representative
     6	# node with every shipped service enabled (bitcoind, lnd, electrs, btcpayserver,
     7	# nbxplorer) and we vulnix its system toplevel — the software a user actually
     8	# runs. (Scanning a test-derivation closure instead would include build-time
     9	# deps like compilers, whose CVEs never reach a deployed node.)
    10	#
    11	# vulnix matches every store path in that closure against the NVD. Findings not
    12	# covered by .github/cve-whitelist.toml are reported; the caller (workflow)
    13	# turns them into a tracking issue.
    14	#
    15	# Usage: ./test/ci/cve-scan.sh [scenario ...]   (default: default regtest)
    16	# Outputs (cwd): vulnix-report.json, cve-summary.md
    17	# Exit: 0 on a completed scan (findings are data, not a failure — the workflow
    18	# decides); nonzero on a FAILED scan (build/vulnix/NVD error), so a broken scan
    19	# fails the job loudly instead of masquerading as "clean" and closing the issue.
    20	set -euo pipefail
    21	
    22	cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}/../.."
    23	REPO=$PWD
    24	WHITELIST="$REPO/.github/cve-whitelist.toml"
    25	NIX=(nix --extra-experimental-features "nix-command flakes")
    26	
    27	# Build a representative node's system toplevel and scan its RUNTIME closure —
    28	# the software a user actually runs. Scanning a test-derivation closure instead
    29	# would drag in build-time deps (go-bootstrap, cargo, shellcheck, …) whose CVEs
    30	# never reach a deployed node and would drown the real findings.
    31	echo "::group::build node toplevel"
    32	toplevel=$("${NIX[@]}" build --no-link --print-out-paths --impure -f test/ci/scan-node.nix) \
    33	  || { echo "node toplevel build failed"; echo '[]' > vulnix-report.json; : > cve-summary.md; exit 1; }
    34	echo "$toplevel"
    35	echo "::endgroup::"
    36	
    37	echo "::group::vulnix"
    38	# vulnix exits non-zero when it finds non-whitelisted vulns; that's expected,
    39	# not an error — capture the JSON regardless.
    40	set +e
    41	"${NIX[@]}" shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#vulnix -c \
    42	  vulnix --json --whitelist "$WHITELIST" "$toplevel" > vulnix-report.json 2> vulnix.err
    43	rc=$?
    44	set -e
    45	cat vulnix.err || true
    46	echo "vulnix exit=$rc"
    47	echo "::endgroup::"
    48	
    49	# vulnix: 0 = clean, 2 = findings, 1 = scan error (NVD fetch, whitelist parse).
    50	# rc=2 is ALSO click's usage-error exit code, so additionally require the output
    51	# to be a JSON array — otherwise an error would read as a clean scan and the
    52	# workflow would close the tracking issue on garbage.
    53	if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && [ "$rc" -ne 2 ]; then
    54	  echo "vulnix scan FAILED (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1
    55	fi
    56	jq -e 'type == "array"' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    57	  || { echo "vulnix produced no valid JSON (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    58	
    59	# Summarize with the runner's native jq (present on GitHub runners and pop-os).
    60	# vulnix --json fields: `affected_by` holds only the ACTIVE (non-whitelisted)
    61	# CVEs — whitelist-masked ones are moved to the `whitelisted` array, and
    62	# derivations whose CVEs are ALL masked are omitted from the JSON entirely.
    63	# So filter on affected_by alone: also requiring `whitelisted` to be empty
    64	# silently dropped any derivation carrying BOTH a scoped-whitelisted collision
    65	# CVE and a genuinely new one — defeating the per-CVE scoping policy.
    66	real='[.[] | select(((.affected_by // []) | length) > 0)]'
    67	maxcvss='(([.cvssv3_basescore // {} | .[]] | max) // 0)'
    68	count=$(jq "$real | length" vulnix-report.json 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
    69	{
    70	  echo "# CVE scan — $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ)"
    71	  echo
    72	  echo "Scanned: representative node toplevel (bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver + nbxplorer), runtime closure"
    73	  echo "Affected store paths with non-whitelisted CVEs: **$count**"
    74	  echo
    75	  if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then
    76	    # shellcheck disable=SC2016  # jq program, not shell — \(...) is jq syntax
    77	    jq -r "$real | map(. + {_m: $maxcvss}) | sort_by(-._m) | .[] | \"## \(.pname // .name) \(.version // \"\") — CVSS \(._m)\n- \((.affected_by // []) | join(\", \"))\"" \
    78	      vulnix-report.json
    79	    echo
    80	    echo "Triage: fix in \`pkgs/overrides.nix\` (version-guarded); whitelist test/build"
    81	    echo "tooling (this scans the full test closure) or N/A CVEs in \`.github/cve-whitelist.toml\`."
    82	  else
    83	    echo "No action needed."
    84	  fi
    85	} > cve-summary.md
    86	
    87	# Emit ONLY the machine-readable result to $GITHUB_OUTPUT (logs go to stdout).
    88	if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then found=true; else found=false; fi
    89	echo "found=$found" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT:-/dev/stdout}"
    90	echo "scan complete: found=$found"
     1	{ config, lib, pkgs, ... }:

     1	# vulnix whitelist for the repo CVE scan (test/ci/cve-scan.sh).
     2	#
     3	# Each entry (a [pname] section) silences findings that are false positives, not
     4	# applicable to how we ship the package, or accepted with a documented reason.
     5	# An entry with no `cve` key whitelists all of that package's current and future
     6	# CVEs — so keep entries NARROW and justified. Format:
     7	# https://github.com/flyingcircusio/vulnix
     8	#
     9	# The scan target (test/ci/scan-node.nix) is a representative node's runtime
    10	# closure. That closure still drags in the BUILD toolchain of some services
    11	# (compilers, build systems, VCS used to fetch sources). Those packages are
    12	# never invoked by a running node service and are not network-reachable, so
    13	# their CVEs are not node attack surface. They are whitelisted below. Everything
    14	# else — glibc, zlib, sqlite, dbus, nghttp2, snappy, openssl, and the like — is
    15	# left REPORTED for real human triage (is our version actually affected? is
    16	# there an upstream fix to pull into pkgs/overrides.nix?).
    17	#
    18	# Reviewed 2026-08-20. Revisit if a service starts invoking one of these at
    19	# runtime (then it becomes attack surface again).
    20	#
    21	# The entries above (build tooling) whitelist a whole pname. The section at the
    22	# BOTTOM is a different category: vulnix pname COLLISIONS — CVEs that belong to
    23	# an unrelated product sharing the package name, and do not apply to the software
    24	# we ship at all. Those are scoped with an explicit `cve = [...]` list (never a
    25	# blanket pname whitelist), so a genuinely-new CVE for the real package still
    26	# reports. Each was verified against NVD on the date noted.
    27	#
    28	# Reviewed 2026-08-24 (collision triage of cve-scan issue #1).
    29	
    30	[gcc]
    31	comment = "compiler in the build closure; not run on a deployed node"
    32	
    33	[go]
    34	comment = "Go toolchain/bootstrap from a service build; not run on a deployed node"
    35	
    36	[cargo]
    37	comment = "Rust build tool (electrs); not run on a deployed node"
    38	
    39	[binutils]
    40	comment = "build toolchain; not run on a deployed node"
    41	
    42	[ninja]
    43	comment = "build system; not run on a deployed node"
    44	
    45	[patch]
    46	comment = "build-time source patching; not run on a deployed node"
    47	
    48	[ada]
    49	comment = "build toolchain; not run on a deployed node"
    50	
    51	[lapack]
    52	comment = "build-time numeric lib; not part of any node service's runtime path"
    53	
    54	[fontforge]
    55	comment = "build-time font tooling (web assets); not run on a deployed node"
    56	
    57	[mercurial]
    58	comment = "VCS used to fetch sources at build time; not run on a deployed node"
    59	
    60	[ShellCheck]
    61	comment = "build/lint tooling; not run on a deployed node"
    62	
    63	[async]
    64	comment = "Haskell build dep (ShellCheck); not run on a deployed node"
    65	
    66	[Diff]
    67	comment = "build-time diff lib; not run on a deployed node"
    68	
    69	[stringbuilder]
    70	comment = "build-time .NET dep (btcpayserver); not run on a deployed node"
    71

53-- **We own the nixpkgs pin now.** Security bumps for kept packages land as
54-  version-guarded overrides in `pkgs/overrides.nix` (inert once the pin catches
55-  up). The pin is moved deliberately and tested (below).
56-- **Every change is tested before `release` moves:** the full NixOS VM assertion
57-  suite (`default`/`regtest`/`netns`) runs in CI on KVM, plus a weekly
58:  [CVE scan](.github/workflows/cve-scan.yml) of the shipped closure. All commits
59-  are signed; the `release` branch only advances on green CI.

   262	          # world-readable through /proc/<pid>/cmdline

   266	            # so this root script must never write/chmod/chown through a path the
   267	            # lnd process could tamper with (else a compromised lnd escalates to
   268	            # arbitrary root file overwrite/chown). Staging in $RUNTIME_DIRECTORY
   269	            # is NOT enough — lnd owns that directory and can unlink our temp file
   270	            # and swap in a symlink before we write to it. Stage instead in a
   271	            # ROOT-ONLY directory (mode 0700, owned by root, that lnd cannot enter
   272	            # or replace) on the SAME tmpfs as /run/lnd, so the final move is a
   273	            # rename(2): atomic, and it replaces the destination without following
   274	            # a symlink lnd may have planted there.
   275	            staging=$(${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mktemp -d "/run/lnd-macaroons.XXXXXX")
   276	            trap '${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/rm -rf "$staging"' EXIT
   277	            ${lib.concatMapStrings (macaroon: ''
   278	              echo "Create custom macaroon ${macaroon}"
   279	              macaroonTmp="$staging/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   280	              # Read admin.macaroon AS the lnd user, never as root: it lives in
   281	              # lnd's own 0770 datadir, so a compromised lnd could plant a
   282	              # symlink there and have this root script dereference it to
   283	              # exfiltrate an arbitrary root-only file (backup passphrase, wg
   284	              # key, host keys) via the REST call below. Reading as the lnd user
   285	              # fails closed on such a symlink — lnd cannot read what lnd cannot
   286	              # read — and grants no access lnd does not already have. (The write
   287	              # path above is already symlink-hardened; this closes the read.)
   288	              adminMacaroonHex=$(${pkgs.util-linux}/bin/runuser -u ${cfg.user} -- ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -ps -u -c 99999 '${networkDir}/admin.macaroon')
   289	              # The response body is lnd-controlled. A macaroon is a few hundred
   290	              # bytes, so cap what this root script will buffer and write to the
   291	              # /run tmpfs at 64 KiB, and fail (jq -e) rather than write garbage
   292	              # when the reply carries no `.macaroon` (audit 2026-08-30, low).
   293	              ${curl} \
   294	                -H @<(printf 'Grpc-Metadata-macaroon: %s\n' "$adminMacaroonHex") \
   295	                -X POST \
   296	                -d '{"permissions":[${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.permissions}]}' \
   297	                ${restUrl}/macaroon |\
   298	                ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/head -c 65536 |\
   299	                ${pkgs.jq}/bin/jq -ce '.macaroon | strings' | ${pkgs.xxd}/bin/xxd -p -r > "$macaroonTmp"
   300	              chown ${cfg.macaroons.${macaroon}.user}: "$macaroonTmp"
   301	              ${pkgs.coreutils}/bin/mv -f "$macaroonTmp" "$RUNTIME_DIRECTORY/${macaroon}.macaroon"
   302	            '') (attrNames cfg.macaroons)}
   303	          '';
   304	        in [
   305	          script
   306	        ];

    98	        makePasswordSecret() {
    99	          # Passwords have alphabet {a-z, A-Z, 0-9} and ~119 bits of entropy
   100	          [[ -e $1 ]] || ${pkgs.pwgen}/bin/pwgen -s 20 1 > "$1"
   101	        }
   102	        makeBitcoinRPCPassword() {
   103	          user=$1
   104	          file=bitcoin-rpcpassword-$user
   105	          HMACfile=bitcoin-HMAC-$user
   106	          makePasswordSecret "$file"
   107	          if [[ $file -nt $HMACfile ]]; then
   108	            ${rpcauth} "$user" "$file" | grep rpcauth | cut -d ':' -f 2 > "$HMACfile"
   109	          fi
   110	        }
   111	        makeCert() {
   112	          name=$1
   113	          # Add leading comma if not empty
   114	          extraAltNames=''${2:+,}''${2:-}
   115	          if [[ ! -e $name-key ]]; then
   116	            # Create new key and cert
   117	            doMakeCert "-newkey ec -pkeyopt ec_paramgen_curve:prime256v1 -nodes -keyout $name-key"
   118	          elif [[ ! -e $name-cert \
   119	                  || $(cat "$name-cert-alt-names" 2>/dev/null) != $extraAltNames ]]; then
   120	            # Create cert from existing key
   121	            doMakeCert "-key $name-key"
   122	          fi;
   123	        }
   124	        doMakeCert() {
   125	           # This fn uses global variables `name` and `extraAltNames`
   126	           keyOpts=$1
   127	           ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl req -x509 \
   128	             -sha256 -days 3650 $keyOpts -out "$name-cert"  \
   129	             -subj "/CN=localhost/O=$name" \
   130	             -addext "subjectAltName=DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1$extraAltNames"
   131	           echo "$extraAltNames" > "$name-cert-alt-names"
   132	        }
   133	
   134	        umask u=rw,go=
   135	        ${builtins.concatStringsSep "\n" (builtins.attrValues cfg.generateSecretsCmds)}

   199	           file="$1"
   200	           user="$2"
   201	           group="$3"
   202	           permissions="$4"
   203	           if [[ ! -e $file ]]; then
   204	             echo "Error: Secret file '$file' is missing"
   205	             exit 1
   206	           fi
   207	           chown "$user:$group" "$file"
   208	           chmod "$permissions" "$file"
   209	           processedFiles+=("$file")
   210	        }
   211	
   212	        dir="${cfg.secretsDir}"
   213	        if [[ ! -e $dir ]]; then
   214	          echo "Error: Secrets dir '$dir' is missing"
   215	          exit 1
   216	        fi
   217	        chown root: "$dir"
   218	        cd "$dir"
   219	
   220	        processedFiles=()
   221	        ${
   222	          concatStrings (mapAttrsToList (n: v: ''
   223	            setupSecret ${escapeShellArg n} ${escapeShellArg v.user} ${escapeShellArg v.group} ${escapeShellArg v.permissions}
   224	          '') cfg.secrets)
   225	        }
   226	
   227	        # Make all other files accessible to root only.
   228	        # dotglob: a stray `.something` in the secrets dir must be locked down
   229	        # too, not skipped by the glob (audit 2026-08-30, low).
   230	        unprocessedFiles=$(
   231	          comm -23 <(shopt -s nullglob dotglob; printf '%s\n' * | sort) <(printf '%s\n' "''${processedFiles[@]}" | sort)
   232	        )
   233	        if [[ $unprocessedFiles ]]; then
   234	          IFS=$'\n'
   235	          # shellcheck disable=SC2086
   236	          chown root: $unprocessedFiles
   237	          # shellcheck disable=SC2086
   238	          chmod 0440 $unprocessedFiles
   239	        fi
   240	
   241	        # Now make the secrets dir accessible to other users
   242	        chmod 0751 "$dir"
   243	      '';
   244	    };
   245	  };
   246	}
     1	{ config, lib, pkgs, ... }:
     2

   107	      enable = true;
   108	      rpc.users.btcpayserver = {
   109	        passwordHMACFromFile = true;
   110	        # nbxplorer/btcpayserver need only the read/relay calls in the public
   111	        # whitelist. The former `setban` + `generatetoaddress` extras were
   112	        # over-grants for the RPC user reachable from the internet-facing
   113	        # btcpayserver: setban lets a web-app compromise eclipse the node
   114	        # (persistent peer bans), and generatetoaddress burns CPU via `maxtries`
   115	        # on mainnet and is regtest-only otherwise. Neither is used by nbxplorer,
   116	        # btcpayserver, or the test suite. Removed in the audit follow-up
   117	        # 2026-08-20; if you run btcpay in regtest and want its "mine" button,
   118	        # grant generatetoaddress in your own config.
   119	        rpcwhitelist = cfg.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist;
   120	        # Note: nbxplorer's Indexer.ConnectNode needs getpeerinfo — it is in the

   175	        } >> '${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config'
   176	      '';
   177	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   178	        ExecStart = ''
   179	          ${cfg.nbxplorer.package}/bin/nbxplorer --conf=${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}/settings.config \
   180	            --datadir=${cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir}
   181	        '';
   182	        User = cfg.nbxplorer.user;
   183	        Restart = "on-failure";
   184	        RestartSec = "10s";
   185	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.nbxplorer.dataDir ];
   186	        # .NET's JIT needs writable+executable pages, so W^X enforcement cannot
   187	        # be enabled for NBXplorer or BTCPay. Accepted trade-off (audit

   212	          "certfilepath=${config.services.lnd.certPath}" +

   217	      requires = [ "postgresql.target" ];
   218	      wants = [ "nbxplorer.service" ]

   227	        # Since 2.4.0, btcpayserver uses `Host.CreateDefaultBuilder`, which sets the

   229	        # The web root (`wwwroot`) is resolved relative to the content root, so

     1	name: Release tag
     2	
     3	# Every push to `release` is a fleet-validated snapshot (the branch ruleset only
     4	# accepts commits with all CI checks green). Tag those snapshots so advisories,
     5	# users, and downstream pins can name a version instead of a bare commit hash.
     6	#
     7	# CalVer (YYYY.MM.DD[.N]): for a security-sensitive fork, "how fresh is this
     8	# pin" is the question a version should answer, and it never collides with
     9	# upstream's frozen v0.0.x line. Only closure-affecting pushes are tagged;
    10	# CI/docs-only fast-forwards are skipped.
    11	on:
    12	  push:
    13	    branches: [release]
    14	
    15	permissions:
    16	  contents: write
    17	
    18	concurrency:
    19	  group: release-tag
    20	  cancel-in-progress: false
    21	
    22	jobs:
    23	  tag:
    24	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    25	    steps:
    26	      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
    27	        with:
    28	          fetch-depth: 0
    29	      - name: Tag and release if the closure changed
    30	        env:
    31	          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
    32	        run: |
    33	          set -euo pipefail
    34	          git config user.name  "github-actions[bot]"
    35	          git config user.email "41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
    36	          prev=$(git describe --tags --abbrev=0 --match '20*' 2>/dev/null || echo "")
    37	
    38	          # Skip when nothing closure-affecting changed since the last release.
    39	          if [ -n "$prev" ]; then
    40	            if git diff --quiet "$prev"..HEAD -- modules pkgs overlay.nix default.nix flake.nix flake.lock; then
    41	              echo "No closure-affecting change since $prev — not tagging."
    42	              exit 0
    43	            fi
    44	          fi
    45	
    46	          date=$(date -u +%Y.%m.%d)
    47	          tag=$date; n=1
    48	          while git rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$tag" >/dev/null; do
    49	            n=$((n+1)); tag="$date.$n"
    50	          done
    51	
    52	          {
    53	            echo "Fleet-validated snapshot of the maintained nix-bitcoin fork."
    54	            echo
    55	            echo "## nixpkgs pins"
    56	            for inp in nixpkgs nixpkgs-unstable; do
    57	              rev=$(jq -r ".nodes.\"$inp\".locked.rev // empty" flake.lock)
    58	              [ -n "$rev" ] && echo "- \`$inp\`: $rev"
    59	            done
    60	            echo
    61	            echo "## Changes since ${prev:-the beginning}"
    62	            if [ -n "$prev" ]; then git log --no-merges --pretty='- %s' "$prev"..HEAD; else echo "- initial tagged release"; fi
    63	          } > notes.md
    64	
    65	          git tag -a "$tag" -m "Release $tag"
    66	          git push origin "refs/tags/$tag"
    67	          gh release create "$tag" --title "$tag" --notes-file notes.md
    68	          echo "Released $tag"
     1	# Security Policy
     2	
     3	This repository is an independently maintained fork of
     4	[fort-nix/nix-bitcoin](https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin), which is no
     5	longer actively maintained. The contacts, GPG keys, signing key, and security
     6	fund listed in upstream's SECURITY.md belong to the original developers and do
     7	not reach or represent this fork. Do not send reports or donations to them on
     8	our behalf.
     9	
    10	## Reporting a Vulnerability
    11	
    12	Report vulnerabilities through [GitHub private vulnerability
    13	reporting](../../security/advisories/new). This opens a private security
    14	advisory that only the maintainers can see.
    15	
    16	If private reporting is unavailable, open a regular issue that says you have a
    17	security concern, without any details, and we will arrange a private channel.
    18	
    19	Please include:
    20	
    21	- The component and version or commit you tested
    22	- Steps to reproduce or a proof of concept
    23	- The impact you see (fund theft, privacy loss, denial of service, ...)
    24	
    25	We aim to acknowledge reports within 7 days. We follow coordinated disclosure:
    26	we agree on a public disclosure date with you, credit you in the fix and
    27	release notes unless you prefer anonymity.
    28	
    29	## Scope
    30	
    31	In scope:
    32	
    33	- nix-bitcoin's own code: NixOS modules, `netns-exec`, secrets handling,
    34	  helper scripts, packaging in `pkgs/`
    35	- Privilege boundaries we claim to provide: service isolation, RPC
    36	  whitelisting, `netns-isolation`, systemd sandboxing
    37	- Documentation that instructs users to do something blatantly insecure
    38	
    39	Out of scope:
    40	
    41	- Vulnerabilities in the daemons we package (bitcoind, lnd,
    42	  electrs, btcpayserver, nbxplorer, ...). Report those to the upstream
    43	  project first. If upstream has shipped a fix that we have not yet pulled in,
    44	  tell us and we will treat it as urgent.
    45	- Attacks requiring physical access or an already-compromised deployment
    46	  machine.
    47	
    48	## Supported Versions
    49	
    50	Only the latest commit on the default branch and the latest release are
    51	supported. Security fixes are delivered by updating your flake input; we do
    52	not maintain stable branches.
    53	
    54	## Security Tracker
    55	
    56	Known findings and their status are tracked in
    57	[docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md](docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md). The
    58	audit is re-run on every nixpkgs pin update.
    59	
    60	## Adversarial review
    61	
    62	Beyond CI's NixOS VM assertion suite, every change is reviewed by two
    63	independent open-weight models (Kimi K3 + GLM-5.2) acting as adversarial
    64	security auditors, at max reasoning. The **methodology, the audit tooling, and
    65	the full results of every run** live on the [`audits`](../../tree/audits)
    66	branch — kept off `master`/`release` so the code you pin stays clean. Browse it
    67	to follow the reviews, read the raw transcripts, or run the suite yourself;
    68	PRs to improve the audit prompt are welcome.
    69	
    70	## Release Integrity
    71	
    72	Commits and tags on this fork are signed with the maintainer's SSH key.
    73	GitHub shows verified commits as "Verified"; to verify locally, add the
    74	maintainer's public signing key to an SSH allowed-signers file and run
    75	`git log --show-signature` or `git verify-commit <rev>`.
    76	
    77	Because this fork is consumed as a flake input, integrity ultimately comes
    78	from your `flake.lock`: it records the exact commit and its content hash
    79	(`narHash`), and Nix refuses any input whose contents do not match. Pin a
    80	specific revision and review changes before advancing it.
    81	
    82	`bitcoind` source tarballs are additionally verified against the Bitcoin
    83	Core `guix.sigs` keyring at build time.
    84	
    85	Note: the upstream tarball-release helper `helper/fetch-release` is not used by
    86	this fork. It fetches upstream fort-nix releases and references the original
    87	maintainer's GPG key. It refuses to run unless `NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1`
    88	is set, and it points at the flake pin instead. Do not rely on it. This fork
    89	releases through CalVer tags on the `release` branch (`release-tag.yml`).
     1	#!/usr/bin/env nix-shell
     2	#!nix-shell -i bash -p bash coreutils curl jq gnupg gnugrep
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	
     5	scriptDir=$(cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}" && pwd)
     6	
     7	trap 'echo "Error at ${BASH_SOURCE[0]}:$LINENO"' ERR
     8	
     9	repo=fort-nix/nix-bitcoin
    10	
    11	# This legacy helper fetches an UPSTREAM (fort-nix) signed release tarball. This
    12	# repository is a maintained FORK; upstream's release artifacts do NOT contain
    13	# this fork's fixes, so fetching them here would silently ship un-fixed code.
    14	# Consume this fork via its flake pin instead:
    15	#   inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = "github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release";
    16	# Refuse to run unless explicitly overridden.
    17	if [[ ! -v NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM ]]; then
    18	    echo "This is a fork of nix-bitcoin; pin the flake, don't fetch releases:" >&2
    19	    echo "  inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = \"github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release\";" >&2
    20	    echo "This helper fetches UPSTREAM ($repo) releases, WITHOUT the fork's fixes." >&2
    21	    echo "Set NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1 only if you truly want upstream." >&2
    22	    exit 1
    23	fi
    24	
    25	if [[ ! -v version ]]; then
    26	    version=$(curl -fsS "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo/releases/latest" | jq -r '.tag_name' | tail -c +2)
    27	fi
    28	
    29	TMPDIR=$(mktemp -d)
    30	trap 'rm -rf $TMPDIR' EXIT
    31	
    32	export GNUPGHOME=$TMPDIR/gpg-home
    33	mkdir -m 700 "$GNUPGHOME"
    34	
    35	# Import key
    36	gpg --import "$scriptDir/key-jonasnick.bin" &> /dev/null
    37	# Check that exactly one key was imported
    38	(($(gpg --list-keys --with-colons | grep -c pub) == 1))
    39	# Verify key fingerprint
    40	gpg --list-keys "36C7 1A37 C9D9 88BD E825 08D9 B1A7 0E4F 8DCD 0366" > /dev/null
    41	
    42	# Fetch nar-hash of release
    43	cd "$TMPDIR"
    44	baseUrl=https://github.com/$repo/releases/download/v$version
    45	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt"
    46	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt.asc"
    47	
    48	# Verify signature for nar-hash
    49	gpg --verify nar-hash.txt.asc &> /dev/null || {
    50	    >&2 echo "Error: Signature verification failed. Please open an issue in the project repository."
    51	    exit 1
    52	}
    53	
    54	>&2 echo "Fetched and verified release $version"
    55	
    56	cat <<EOF
    57	builtins.fetchTarball {
    58	  url = "https://github.com/$repo/archive/v$version.tar.gz";
    59	  sha256 = "$(cat nar-hash.txt)";
    60	}

    22	      Run krops-deploy and eval-config in parallel.
    23	      This ensures that eval failures appear quickly when deploying.

    27	      Deploy your node via krops

    31

    33	       Build your node system on your local machine
    34	
    35	    generate-secrets
    36	      Create secrets required by your node configuration.
    37	      Secrets are written to ./secrets/
    38	      This function is automatically called by krops-deploy.
    39	
    40	    update-nix-bitcoin
    41	      Fetch and use the latest version of nix-bitcoin
    42	  '';
    43	
    44	  shellHook = ''
    45	    export NIX_PATH="nixpkgs=${nixpkgs}:nix-bitcoin=${toString ../.}:."
    46	    ${setPath}

    48	    export nixpkgsUnstable="${pinned.nixpkgs-unstable}"
    49

    51	    # 1. stdout is a TTY, i.e. we're not piping the output
    52	    # 2. the shell is interactive
    53	    if [[ -t 1 && $- == *i* ]]; then isInteractive=1; else isInteractive=; fi
    54

    56	    export -n helpMessage
    57

    59	    h() { help; }

    62	      ${toString ./fetch-release}

    65	    update-nix-bitcoin() {(
    66	      set -euo pipefail
    67	      releaseFile="${cfgDir}/nix-bitcoin-release.nix"
    68	      current=$(cat "$releaseFile" 2>/dev/null || true)
    69	      new=$(fetch-release)
    70	      if [[ $new == $current ]]; then
    71	        echo "nix-bitcoin-release.nix already contains the latest release"
    72	      else
    73	        echo "$new" > "$releaseFile"
    74	        echo "Updated nix-bitcoin-release.nix"
    75	        if [[ $isInteractive ]]; then
    76	          exec nix-shell
    77	        fi
    78	      fi
    79	    )}
    80	
    81	    generate-secrets() {(
    82	      set -euo pipefail
    83	      config="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix"
    84	      if [[ ! -e $config ]]; then
    85	        config="${cfgDir}/configuration.nix"
    86	      fi
    87	      genSecrets=$(nix-build --no-out-link -I nixos-config="$config" \
    88	                   '<nixpkgs/nixos>' -A config.nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsScript)
    89	      mkdir -p "${cfgDir}/secrets"
    90	      (cd "${cfgDir}/secrets"; $genSecrets)
    91	    )}

    93	    deploy() {(
    94	      set -euo pipefail
    95	      krops-deploy &
    96	      kropsPid=$!
    97	      if eval-config; then
    98	        wait $kropsPid
    99	      else
   100	        # Kill all subprocesses
   101	        kill $(pidClosure $kropsPid)
   102	        return 1
   103	      fi
   104	    )}

   106	    krops-deploy() {(

   108	      generate-secrets

   110	      # the target machine
   111	      chmod 700 "${cfgDir}/secrets"
   112	      $(nix-build --no-out-link "${cfgDir}/krops/deploy.nix")

   119	        nix-instantiate --eval ${nixpkgs}/nixos $system -A system.outPath | tr -d '"'
   120	      echo
   121	    )}
   122

   124	      set -euo pipefail
   125	      system=$(getNodeSystem)

   170	
   171	## 2. Nix installation
   172	The following steps are meant to be run on the machine you deploy from, not the machine you deploy to.
   173	You can also build Nix from source by following the instructions at https://nixos.org/nix/manual/#ch-installing-source.
   174	
   175	1. Install Dependencies (Debian 10 Buster)
   176	
   177	    ```
   178	    sudo apt-get install curl git gnupg2 dirmngr
   179	    ```
   180	
   181	2. Install a current multi-user Nix. Follow the official installer and its
   182	   verification steps at https://nixos.org/download.
   183	
   184	    ```
   185	    curl -L https://nixos.org/nix/install | sh -s -- --daemon
   186	    ```
   187	
   188	    This fork is a flake. Enable the flake features. Add the line
   189	    `experimental-features = nix-command flakes` to `/etc/nix/nix.conf`, then
   190	    restart the `nix-daemon`. Open a new terminal window when you are done.
   191	
   192	    If you get an error similar to
   193	
   194	    ```
   195	    error: cloning builder process: Operation not permitted
   196	    error: unable to start build process
   197	    /tmp/nix-binary-tarball-unpack.hqawN4uSPr/unpack/nix-2.2.1-x86_64-linux/install: unable to install Nix into your default profile
   198	    ```
   199	
   200	    you're likely not installing as multi-user because you forgot to pass the `--daemon` flag to the install script.
   201	
   202	3. Optional: Disallow substitutes
   203	
   204	    You can put `substitute = false` to your `nix.conf` usually found in `/etc/nix/` to build the packages from source.
   205	    This eliminates an attack vector where nix's build server or binary cache is compromised.
   206	
   207	## 3. Setup deployment directory
   208	
   209	1. Clone this project
   210	
   211	    ```
   212	    git clone https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin
   213	    ```
   214	
   215	2. Create a new directory for your nix-bitcoin node config and copy initial files from nix-bitcoin
   216	
   217	    ```
   218	    mkdir nix-bitcoin-node
   219	    cd nix-bitcoin-node
   220	    cp -r ../nix-bitcoin/examples/{configuration.nix,shell.nix,krops,.gitignore} .
   221	    ```
   222	
   223	3. Obtain the hash of the latest nix-bitcoin release
   224	
   225	    ```
   226	    ../nix-bitcoin/helper/fetch-release > nix-bitcoin-release.nix
   227	    ```
   228	
   229	#### Optional: Specify the system of your node
   230	This enables evaluating your node config on a machine that has a different system platform
   231	than your node.\
   232	Examples: Deploying from macOS or deploying from a x86 desktop PC to a Raspberry Pi.
   233	
   234	```bash
   235	# Run this when your node has a 64-Bit x86 CPU (e.g., an Intel or AMD CPU)
   236	echo "x86_64-linux" > krops/system
   237	
   238	# Run this when your node has a 64-Bit ARM CPU (e.g., Raspberry Pi 4 B, Pine64)
   239	echo "aarch64-linux" > krops/system
   240	```
   241	Other available systems:
   242	- `i686-linux` (`x86`)
   243	- `armv7l-linux` (`ARMv7`)\
   244	  This platform is untested and has no binary caches.
   245	  [See here](https://nixos.wiki/wiki/NixOS_on_ARM) for details.
   246	
   247	## 4. Deploy with krops
   248	
   249	1. Edit your ssh config
   250	
   251	    ```
   252	    nano ~/.ssh/config
   253	    ```
   254	
   255	    and add the node with an entry similar to the following (make sure to fix `Hostname` and `IdentityFile`):
   256	
   257	    ```
   258	    Host bitcoin-node
   259	        # FIXME
   260	        Hostname NODE_IP_ADDRESS_OR_HOST_NAME_HERE
   261	        User root
   262	        PubkeyAuthentication yes
   263	        # FIXME
   264	        IdentityFile ~/.ssh/id_...
   265	        AddKeysToAgent yes
   266	    ```
   267	
   268	2. Make sure you are in the deployment directory and edit `krops/deploy.nix`
   269	
   270	    ```
   271	    nano krops/deploy.nix
   272	    ```
   273	
   274	    Locate the `FIXME` and set the target to the name of the ssh config entry created earlier, i.e. `bitcoin-node`.
   275	
   276	    Note that any file imported by your `configuration.nix` must be copied to the target machine by krops.
   277	    For example, if there is an import of `networking.nix` you must add it to `extraSources` in `krops/deploy.nix` like this:
   278	    ```
   279	    extraSources = {
   280	        "hardware-configuration.nix".file = toString ../hardware-configuration.nix;
   281	        "networking.nix".file = toString ../networking.nix;
   282	    };
   283	    ```
   284	
   285	3. Optional: Disallow substitutes
   286	
   287	    If you prefer to build the system from source instead of copying binaries from the Nix cache, add the following line to `configuration.nix`:
   288	    ```
   289	    nix.extraOptions = "substitute = false";
   290	    ```

    23	
    24	See [Nix - A One Pager](https://github.com/tazjin/nix-1p) for a short guide
    25	to Nix, the language used in `configuration.nix`.\
    26	You can follow along this guide by running command `nix repl` which allows you to interactively

    31	## Set options
    32	
    33	All features and services are configurable through options. You can find a list of
    34	supported options at the top of each nix-bitcoin [module](../modules/modules.nix)
    35	(Examples: [bitcoind.nix](../modules/bitcoind.nix), [btcpayserver.nix](../modules/btcpayserver.nix)).
    36	
    37	Example: Set some `bitcoind` options by adding these lines to your `configuration.nix`:
    38	```nix
    39	# Use a custom data dir
    40	services.bitcoind.dataDir = "/my/datadir";
    41	
    42	# Enable txindex
    43	services.bitcoind.txindex = true;
    44	```
    45	
    46	You can also use regular [NixOS options](https://search.nixos.org/options)
    47	for configuring your system:
    48	```nix
    49	networking.hostName = "myhost";
    50	time.timeZone = "UTC";
    51	```
    52	
    53	## Debug your configuration
    54	
    55	To print the values of specific options of your config, add the following to your `configuration.nix`:
    56	```nix
    57	system.extraDependencies = let
    58	  debugVal = config.networking.firewall.allowedTCPPorts;
    59	  # More example options:
    60	  # debugVal = config.environment.systemPackages;
    61	  # debugVal = config.services.bitcoind.dataDir;
    62	in lib.traceSeqN 3 debugVal [];
    63	```
    64	and run command `eval-config` from the deployment shell.

    73	  listen = true;

    77

    88	};
