 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	name: Release tag
     2	
     3	# Every push to `release` is a fleet-validated snapshot (the branch ruleset only
     4	# accepts commits with all CI checks green). Tag those snapshots so advisories,
     5	# users, and downstream pins can name a version instead of a bare commit hash.
     6	#
     7	# CalVer (YYYY.MM.DD[.N]): for a security-sensitive fork, "how fresh is this
     8	# pin" is the question a version should answer, and it never collides with
     9	# upstream's frozen v0.0.x line. Only closure-affecting pushes are tagged;
    10	# CI/docs-only fast-forwards are skipped.
    11	on:
    12	  push:
    13	    branches: [release]
    14	
    15	permissions:
    16	  contents: write
    17	
    18	concurrency:
    19	  group: release-tag
    20	  cancel-in-progress: false
    21	
    22	jobs:
    23	  tag:
    24	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    25	    steps:
    26	      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
    27	        with:
    28	          fetch-depth: 0
    29	      - name: Tag and release if the closure changed
    30	        env:
    31	          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
    32	        run: |
    33	          set -euo pipefail
    34	          git config user.name  "github-actions[bot]"
    35	          git config user.email "41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
    36	          prev=$(git describe --tags --abbrev=0 --match '20*' 2>/dev/null || echo "")
    37	
    38	          # Skip when nothing closure-affecting changed since the last release.
    39	          if [ -n "$prev" ]; then
    40	            if git diff --quiet "$prev"..HEAD -- modules pkgs overlay.nix default.nix flake.nix flake.lock; then
    41	              echo "No closure-affecting change since $prev — not tagging."
    42	              exit 0
    43	            fi
    44	          fi
    45	
    46	          date=$(date -u +%Y.%m.%d)
    47	          tag=$date; n=1
    48	          while git rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$tag" >/dev/null; do
    49	            n=$((n+1)); tag="$date.$n"
    50	          done
    51	
    52	          {
    53	            echo "Fleet-validated snapshot of the maintained nix-bitcoin fork."
    54	            echo
    55	            echo "## nixpkgs pins"
    56	            for inp in nixpkgs nixpkgs-unstable; do
    57	              rev=$(jq -r ".nodes.\"$inp\".locked.rev // empty" flake.lock)
    58	              [ -n "$rev" ] && echo "- \`$inp\`: $rev"
    59	            done
    60	            echo
    61	            echo "## Changes since ${prev:-the beginning}"
    62	            if [ -n "$prev" ]; then git log --no-merges --pretty='- %s' "$prev"..HEAD; else echo "- initial tagged release"; fi
    63	          } > notes.md
    64	
    65	          git tag -a "$tag" -m "Release $tag"
    66	          git push origin "refs/tags/$tag"
    67	          gh release create "$tag" --title "$tag" --notes-file notes.md
    68	          echo "Released $tag"
     1	# Security Policy
     2	
     3	This repository is an independently maintained fork of
     4	[fort-nix/nix-bitcoin](https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin), which is no
     5	longer actively maintained. The contacts, GPG keys, signing key, and security
     6	fund listed in upstream's SECURITY.md belong to the original developers and do
     7	not reach or represent this fork. Do not send reports or donations to them on
     8	our behalf.
     9	
    10	## Reporting a Vulnerability
    11	
    12	Report vulnerabilities through [GitHub private vulnerability
    13	reporting](../../security/advisories/new). This opens a private security
    14	advisory that only the maintainers can see.
    15	
    16	If private reporting is unavailable, open a regular issue that says you have a
    17	security concern, without any details, and we will arrange a private channel.
    18	
    19	Please include:
    20	
    21	- The component and version or commit you tested
    22	- Steps to reproduce or a proof of concept
    23	- The impact you see (fund theft, privacy loss, denial of service, ...)
    24	
    25	We aim to acknowledge reports within 7 days. We follow coordinated disclosure:
    26	we agree on a public disclosure date with you, credit you in the fix and
    27	release notes unless you prefer anonymity.
    28	
    29	## Scope
    30	
    31	In scope:
    32	
    33	- nix-bitcoin's own code: NixOS modules, `netns-exec`, secrets handling,
    34	  helper scripts, packaging in `pkgs/`
    35	- Privilege boundaries we claim to provide: service isolation, RPC
    36	  whitelisting, `netns-isolation`, systemd sandboxing
    37	- Documentation that instructs users to do something blatantly insecure
    38	
    39	Out of scope:
    40	
    41	- Vulnerabilities in the daemons we package (bitcoind, lnd,
    42	  electrs, btcpayserver, nbxplorer, ...). Report those to the upstream
    43	  project first. If upstream has shipped a fix that we have not yet pulled in,
    44	  tell us and we will treat it as urgent.
    45	- Attacks requiring physical access or an already-compromised deployment
    46	  machine.
    47	
    48	## Supported Versions
    49	
    50	Only the latest commit on the default branch and the latest release are
    51	supported. Security fixes are delivered by updating your flake input; we do
    52	not maintain stable branches.
    53	
    54	## Security Tracker
    55	
    56	Known findings and their status are tracked in
    57	[docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md](docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md). The
    58	audit is re-run on every nixpkgs pin update.
    59	
    60	## Adversarial review
    61	
    62	Beyond CI's NixOS VM assertion suite, every change is reviewed by two
    63	independent open-weight models (Kimi K3 + GLM-5.2) acting as adversarial
    64	security auditors, at max reasoning. The **methodology, the audit tooling, and
    65	the full results of every run** live on the [`audits`](../../tree/audits)
    66	branch — kept off `master`/`release` so the code you pin stays clean. Browse it
    67	to follow the reviews, read the raw transcripts, or run the suite yourself;
    68	PRs to improve the audit prompt are welcome.
    69	
    70	## Release Integrity
    71	
    72	Commits and tags on this fork are signed with the maintainer's SSH key.
    73	GitHub shows verified commits as "Verified"; to verify locally, add the
    74	maintainer's public signing key to an SSH allowed-signers file and run
    75	`git log --show-signature` or `git verify-commit <rev>`.
    76	
    77	Because this fork is consumed as a flake input, integrity ultimately comes
    78	from your `flake.lock`: it records the exact commit and its content hash
    79	(`narHash`), and Nix refuses any input whose contents do not match. Pin a
    80	specific revision and review changes before advancing it.
    81	
    82	`bitcoind` source tarballs are additionally verified against the Bitcoin
    83	Core `guix.sigs` keyring at build time.
    84	
    85	Note: the upstream tarball-release helper `helper/fetch-release` is not used by
    86	this fork. It fetches upstream fort-nix releases and references the original
    87	maintainer's GPG key. It refuses to run unless `NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1`
    88	is set, and it points at the flake pin instead. Do not rely on it. This fork
    89	releases through CalVer tags on the `release` branch (`release-tag.yml`).
     1	#!/usr/bin/env nix-shell
     2	#!nix-shell -i bash -p bash coreutils curl jq gnupg gnugrep
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	
     5	scriptDir=$(cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}" && pwd)
     6	
     7	trap 'echo "Error at ${BASH_SOURCE[0]}:$LINENO"' ERR
     8	
     9	repo=fort-nix/nix-bitcoin
    10	
    11	# This legacy helper fetches an UPSTREAM (fort-nix) signed release tarball. This
    12	# repository is a maintained FORK; upstream's release artifacts do NOT contain
    13	# this fork's fixes, so fetching them here would silently ship un-fixed code.
    14	# Consume this fork via its flake pin instead:
    15	#   inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = "github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release";
    16	# Refuse to run unless explicitly overridden.
    17	if [[ ! -v NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM ]]; then
    18	    echo "This is a fork of nix-bitcoin; pin the flake, don't fetch releases:" >&2
    19	    echo "  inputs.nix-bitcoin.url = \"github:ostermayer/nix-bitcoin/release\";" >&2
    20	    echo "This helper fetches UPSTREAM ($repo) releases, WITHOUT the fork's fixes." >&2
    21	    echo "Set NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1 only if you truly want upstream." >&2
    22	    exit 1
    23	fi
    24	
    25	if [[ ! -v version ]]; then
    26	    version=$(curl -fsS "https://api.github.com/repos/$repo/releases/latest" | jq -r '.tag_name' | tail -c +2)
    27	fi
    28	
    29	TMPDIR=$(mktemp -d)
    30	trap 'rm -rf $TMPDIR' EXIT
    31	
    32	export GNUPGHOME=$TMPDIR/gpg-home
    33	mkdir -m 700 "$GNUPGHOME"
    34	
    35	# Import key
    36	gpg --import "$scriptDir/key-jonasnick.bin" &> /dev/null
    37	# Check that exactly one key was imported
    38	(($(gpg --list-keys --with-colons | grep -c pub) == 1))
    39	# Verify key fingerprint
    40	gpg --list-keys "36C7 1A37 C9D9 88BD E825 08D9 B1A7 0E4F 8DCD 0366" > /dev/null
    41	
    42	# Fetch nar-hash of release
    43	cd "$TMPDIR"
    44	baseUrl=https://github.com/$repo/releases/download/v$version
    45	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt"
    46	curl -fsS -L -O "$baseUrl/nar-hash.txt.asc"
    47	
    48	# Verify signature for nar-hash
    49	gpg --verify nar-hash.txt.asc &> /dev/null || {
    50	    >&2 echo "Error: Signature verification failed. Please open an issue in the project repository."
    51	    exit 1
    52	}
    53	
    54	>&2 echo "Fetched and verified release $version"
    55	
    56	cat <<EOF
    57	builtins.fetchTarball {
    58	  url = "https://github.com/$repo/archive/v$version.tar.gz";
    59	  sha256 = "$(cat nar-hash.txt)";
    60	}
    61	EOF

     1	from collections import OrderedDict
     2	import json
     3	import re
     4	
     5	def succeed(*cmds):
     6	    """Returns the concatenated output of all cmds"""
     7	    return machine.succeed(*cmds)
     8	
     9	def assert_matches(cmd, regexp):
    10	    assert_str_matches(succeed(cmd), regexp)
    11	
    12	def assert_str_matches(str, regexp):
    13	    if not re.search(regexp, str):
    14	        raise Exception(f"Pattern '{regexp}' not found in '{str}'")
    15	
    16	def assert_full_match(cmd, regexp):
    17	    out = succeed(cmd)
    18	    if not re.fullmatch(regexp, out):
    19	        raise Exception(f"Pattern '{regexp}' doesn't match '{out}'")
    20	
    21	def log_has_string(unit, str):
    22	    return f"journalctl -b --output=cat -u {unit} --grep='{str}'"
    23	
    24	def assert_no_failure(unit):
    25	    """Unit should not have failed since the system is running"""
    26	    machine.fail(log_has_string(unit, "Failed with result"))
    27	
    28	def assert_running(unit):
    29	    with machine.nested(f"waiting for unit: {unit}"):
    30	        machine.wait_for_unit(unit)
    31	    assert_no_failure(unit)
    32	
    33	def wait_for_open_port(address, port):
    34	    def is_port_open(_):
    35	        status, _ = machine.execute(f"nc -z {address} {port}")
    36	        return status == 0
    37	
    38	    with machine.nested(f"Waiting for TCP port {address}:{port}"):
    39	        retry(is_port_open)
    40	
    41	
    42	### Test runner
    43	
    44	tests = OrderedDict()
    45	
    46	def test(name):
    47	    def x(fn):
    48	        tests[name] = fn
    49	    return x
    50	
    51	# `run_tests` is already defined by the NixOS test driver
    52	def nb_run_tests():
    53	    enabled = enabled_tests.copy()
    54	    to_run = []
    55	    for test in tests:
    56	        if test in enabled:
    57	            enabled.remove(test)
    58	            to_run.append(test)
    59	    if enabled:
    60	        raise RuntimeError(f"The following tests are enabled but not defined: {enabled}")
    61	    machine.connect()  # Visually separate boot output from the test output
    62	    for test in to_run:
    63	        with machine.nested(f"test: {test}"):
    64	            tests[test]()
    65	
    66	def run_test(test):
    67	    tests[test]()
    68	
    69	
    70	### Tests
    71	# All tests are executed in the order they are defined here
    72	
    73	@test("security")
    74	def _():
    75	    assert_running("setup-secrets")
    76	    # Unused secrets should be inaccessible
    77	    succeed('[[ $(stat -c "%U:%G %a" /secrets/dummy) = "root:root 440" ]]')
    78	
    79	    if "secure-node" in enabled_tests:
    80	        machine.wait_for_unit("bitcoind")
    81	        # `systemctl status` run by unprivileged users shouldn't leak cgroup info
    82	        assert_matches(
    83	            "runuser -u electrs -- systemctl status bitcoind 2>&1 >/dev/null",
    84	            "Failed to dump process list for 'bitcoind.service', ignoring: Access denied",
    85	        )
    86	        # The 'operator' with group 'proc' has full access
    87	        assert_full_match("runuser -u operator -- systemctl status bitcoind 2>&1 >/dev/null", "")

   102	    machine.wait_until_succeeds(
   103	        log_has_string("bitcoind", "RPC User public not allowed to call method stop")

   134	@test("lnd-macaroon-read-deprivileged")
   135	def _():
   136	    # Regression test for the 2026-08-30 audit finding (modules/lnd.nix): the
   137	    # root ExecStartPost that mints custom macaroons must read admin.macaroon AS
   138	    # THE LND USER, never as root. admin.macaroon sits in lnd's own 0770 datadir,
   139	    # so a compromised lnd can replace it with a symlink to a root-only file; if
   140	    # root dereferences it, the bytes are exfiltrated to lnd via the REST call.
   141	    # Reading as the lnd user fails closed on such a symlink.
   142	    assert_running("lnd")
   143	
   144	    # (a) Pin the deployed unit: the macaroon read must go through the run-as-lnd
   145	    #     wrapper. Reverting the fix to a bare root `xxd ... admin.macaroon`
   146	    #     makes this grep fail.
   147	    script = succeed(
   148	        "systemctl show lnd -p ExecStartPost --value | "
   149	        "grep -oE '/nix/store/[a-z0-9]+-lnd-create-macaroons' | head -1"
   150	    ).strip()
   151	    assert script, "lnd-create-macaroons ExecStartPost script not found"
   152	    assert_matches(
   153	        f"cat {script}",
   154	        r"runuser -u lnd [^\n]*xxd[^\n]*admin\.macaroon",
   155	    )
   156	
   157	    # (b) Prove the runtime asymmetry the fix relies on, in lnd's real datadir,
   158	    #     as the real lnd user, without disrupting the service. The canary sits
   159	    #     in a traversable dir (/run) but is root-only readable, mirroring the
   160	    #     real targets (backup passphrase, wg key, host keys).
   161	    succeed(
   162	        "printf ROOT_ONLY_CANARY > /run/macaroon-canary",
   163	        "chown root:root /run/macaroon-canary",
   164	        "chmod 400 /run/macaroon-canary",
   165	    )
   166	    link = "/var/lib/lnd/attack-test-link"
   167	    # The attacker is the lnd user, which owns its 0770 datadir.
   168	    succeed(f"runuser -u lnd -- ln -sf /run/macaroon-canary {link}")
   169	    # Root WOULD read the canary through the symlink (the exploit precondition).
   170	    assert_matches(f"cat {link}", "ROOT_ONLY_CANARY")
   171	    # The lnd user must NOT be able to — so the fix's read fails closed.
   172	    machine.fail(f"runuser -u lnd -- cat {link}")
   173	    succeed(f"rm -f {link} /run/macaroon-canary")
   174

   212	# Run this test before the following tests that shut down services
   213	# (and their corresponding network namespaces).
   214	@test("netns-isolation")
   215	def _():
   216	    def get_ips(services):
   217	        enabled = enabled_tests.intersection(services)
   218	        return " ".join(ip(service) for service in enabled)
   219	
   220	    def assert_reachable(src, dests):
   221	        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
   222	        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
   223	            machine.succeed(f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 {dest_ips}")
   224	
   225	    def assert_unreachable(src, dests):
   226	        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
   227	        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
   228	            machine.fail(
   229	                # This fails when no host is reachable within 100 ms
   230	                f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 --reachable=1 {dest_ips}"
   231	            )
   232	
   233	    # These reachability tests are non-exhaustive
   234	    assert_reachable("bitcoind", ["clightning", "lnd", "liquidd"])
   235	    assert_unreachable("bitcoind", ["btcpayserver", "rtl", "lightning-loop"])
   236	    assert_unreachable("btcpayserver", ["bitcoind", "lightning-loop"])
   237	
   238	    # netns addresses can not be bound to in the main netns.
   239	    # This prevents processes in the main netns from impersonating nix-bitcoin services.
   240	    assert_matches(
   240	    assert_matches(
   241	        f"nc -l {ip('bitcoind')} 1080 2>&1 || true", "nc: Cannot assign requested address"
   242	    )
   243	
   244	    # netns-exec should fail for unauthorized namespaces
   245	    assert_matches(
   246	        "runuser -u operator -- netns-exec nb-clightning ip a 2>&1 || true",
   247	        "nb-clightning is not an allowed netns",
   248	    )
   249	
   250	    # netns-exec should only be executable by the operator user.
   251	    # User `unauthorized` is a member of group `users`, like all normal users.
   252	    # Netns `nb-clightning` is rejected by netns-exec before it accesses the
   253	    # netns, so the error below can only originate from the exec permissions.
   254	    assert_matches(
   255	        "runuser -u unauthorized -- netns-exec nb-clightning ip a 2>&1 || true",
   256	        "Permission denied",
   257	    )
   258	
   259	    if "joinmarket" in enabled_tests:
   260	        # netns-exec should drop capabilities
   261	        assert_matches(
   262	            "runuser -u operator -- netns-exec nb-joinmarket capsh --print | grep Current",
   263	            re.compile("^Current: =$", re.MULTILINE),
   264	        )

   310	def _():
   311	    def enabled(unit):
   312	        if unit in enabled_tests:
   313	            # Wait because the unit might have been restarted in the preceding
   314	            # 'restart-bitcoind' test
   315	            machine.wait_for_unit(unit)
   316	            return True
   317	        else:
   318	            return False

   320	    def get_block_height(ip, port):
   321	        return (
   322	            """echo '{"method": "blockchain.headers.subscribe", "id": 0}'"""
   323	            f" | nc {ip} {port} | head -1 | jq -M .result.height"
   324	        )

   329	        machine.wait_until_succeeds(log_has_string("electrs", "serving Electrum RPC"))

   357

   212	# Run this test before the following tests that shut down services
   213	# (and their corresponding network namespaces).
   214	@test("netns-isolation")
   215	def _():
   216	    def get_ips(services):
   217	        enabled = enabled_tests.intersection(services)
   218	        return " ".join(ip(service) for service in enabled)
   219	
   220	    def assert_reachable(src, dests):
   221	        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
   222	        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
   223	            machine.succeed(f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 {dest_ips}")
   224	
   225	    def assert_unreachable(src, dests):
   226	        dest_ips = get_ips(dests)
   227	        if src in enabled_tests and dest_ips:
   228	            machine.fail(
   229	                # This fails when no host is reachable within 100 ms
   230	                f"ip netns exec nb-{src} fping -c1 -t100 --reachable=1 {dest_ips}"
   231	            )
   232	
   233	    # These reachability tests are non-exhaustive
   234	    assert_reachable("bitcoind", ["clightning", "lnd", "liquidd"])
   235	    assert_unreachable("bitcoind", ["btcpayserver", "rtl", "lightning-loop"])
   236	    assert_unreachable("btcpayserver", ["bitcoind", "lightning-loop"])
   237	
   238	    # netns addresses can not be bound to in the main netns.
   239	    # This prevents processes in the main netns from impersonating nix-bitcoin services.
   240	    assert_matches(
   241	        f"nc -l {ip('bitcoind')} 1080 2>&1 || true", "nc: Cannot assign requested address"
   242	    )

     1	/* This program requires CAP_SYS_ADMIN */
     2	
     3	#define _GNU_SOURCE
     4	#include <sched.h>
     5	#include <stdio.h>
     6	#include <errno.h>
     7	#include <string.h>
     8	#include <unistd.h>
     9	#include <fcntl.h>
    10	#include <sys/capability.h>
    11	
    12	/* Netns names of the services this fork ships (see modules/netns-isolation.nix) */
    13	static char *allowed_netns[] = {
    14	    "nb-bitcoind",
    15	    "nb-lnd",
    16	    "nb-electrs",
    17	    "nb-nbxplorer",
    18	    "nb-btcpayserver"
    19	};
    20	
    21	int is_netns_allowed(char *netns) {
    22	    int n_allowed_netns = sizeof(allowed_netns) / sizeof(allowed_netns[0]);
    23	    for (int i = 0; i < n_allowed_netns; i++) {
    24	        if (strcmp(allowed_netns[i], netns) == 0) {
    25	            return 1;
    26	        }
    27	    }
    28	    return 0;
    29	}
    30	
    31	void print_capabilities() {
    32	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    33	    printf("Capabilities: %s\n", cap_to_text(caps, NULL));
    34	    cap_free(caps);
    35	}
    36	
    37	int drop_capabilities() {
    38	    cap_t caps = cap_get_proc();
    39	    if (caps == NULL) return -1;
    40	    if (cap_clear(caps) < 0) { cap_free(caps); return -1; }
    41	    int rc = cap_set_proc(caps);
    42	    cap_free(caps);
    43	    return rc;
    44	}
    45	
    46	int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    47	    char netns_path[256];
    48	
    49	    if (argc < 3) {
    50	        printf("usage: %s <netns> <command>\n", argv[0]);
    51	        return 1;
    52	    }
    53	
    54	    if (!is_netns_allowed(argv[1])) {
    55	        printf("%s is not an allowed netns.\n", argv[1]);
    56	        return 1;
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    /* Require an absolute path so PATH cannot redirect the exec target.
    60	       The caller's environment is otherwise inherited; the operator who may
    61	       run this wrapper is already root-equivalent. */
    62	    if (argv[2][0] != '/') {
    63	        printf("command must be an absolute path.\n");
    64	        return 1;
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    int len = snprintf(netns_path, sizeof(netns_path), "/var/run/netns/%s", argv[1]);
    68	    if (len < 0 || (size_t)len >= sizeof(netns_path)) {
    69	        printf("Path length exceeded for netns %s.\n", argv[1]);
    70	        return 1;
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    int fd = open(netns_path, O_RDONLY);
    74	    if (fd < 0) {
    75	        printf("Failed opening netns %s: %d, %s \n", netns_path, errno, strerror(errno));
    76	        return 1;
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    if (setns(fd, CLONE_NEWNET) < 0) {
    80	        printf("Failed setns %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    81	        close(fd);
    82	        return 1;
    83	    }
    84	    close(fd);
    85	
    86	    /* Drop capabilities */
    87	    #ifdef DEBUG
    88	    print_capabilities();
    89	    #endif
    90	    if (drop_capabilities() < 0) {
    91	        printf("Failed to drop capabilities: %d, %s \n", errno, strerror(errno));
    92	        return 1;
    93	    }
    94	    #ifdef DEBUG
    95	    print_capabilities();
    96	    #endif
    97	
    98	    execv(argv[2], &argv[2]);
    99	    /* Only reached on exec failure */
   100	    printf("Failed to exec %s: %d, %s \n", argv[2], errno, strerror(errno));
   101	    return 1;
   102	}
     1	qemuDir=$(cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}" && pwd)
     2	
     3	# shellcheck disable=SC1091
     4	source "$qemuDir/wait-until.sh"
     5	
     6	tmpDir=/tmp/nix-bitcoin-qemu-vm
     7	mkdir -p "$tmpDir"
     8	
     9	# Cleanup on exit
    10	cleanup() {
    11	    set +eu
    12	    if [[ $qemuPID ]]; then
    13	        kill -9 "$qemuPID"
    14	    fi
    15	    rm -rf "$tmpDir"
    16	}
    17	trap "cleanup" EXIT
    18	
    19	identityFile=$qemuDir/id-vm
    20	chmod 0600 "$identityFile"
    21	
    22	runVM() {
    23	    vm=$1
    24	    vmNumCPUs=$2
    25	    vmMemoryMiB=$3
    26	    sshPort=$4
    27	
    28	    export NIX_DISK_IMAGE="$tmpDir/img"
    29	    export QEMU_NET_OPTS="hostfwd=tcp::${sshPort}-:22"
    30	    # shellcheck disable=SC2211
    31	    </dev/null "$vm"/bin/run-*-vm -m "$vmMemoryMiB" -smp "$vmNumCPUs" &>/dev/null &
    32	    qemuPID=$!
    33	}
    34	
    35	vmWaitForSSH() {
    36	    echo
    37	    printf "Waiting for SSH connection..."
    38	    waitUntil "c : 2>/dev/null" 500
    39	    echo
    40	}
    41	
    42	# Run command in VM
    43	c() {
    44	    ssh -p "$sshPort" -i "$identityFile" -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o ConnectTimeout=1 \
    45	        -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o LogLevel=ERROR \
    46	        -o ControlMaster=auto -o ControlPath=$tmpDir/ssh-connection -o ControlPersist=60 \
    47	        root@127.0.0.1 "$@"
    48	}
    49	export identityFile
    50	export sshPort
    51	export tmpDir
    52	export -f c

    11	    users.users.root = {
    12	      openssh.authorizedKeys.keyFiles = [ ./id-vm.pub ];
    13	    };
    14	  };
    15	}

480-.PP
481-\fIRemainAfterExit=\fR
482-.RS 4
483-Takes a boolean value that specifies whether the service shall be considered active even when all its processes exited\&. Defaults to
484-\fBno\fR\&.
485-.RE
486-.PP
487-\fIGuessMainPID=\fR
488-.RS 4
489-Takes a boolean value that specifies whether systemd should try to guess the main PID of a service if it cannot be determined reliably\&. This option is ignored unless
490-\fBType=forking\fR
491-is set and
492-\fBPIDFile=\fR
493-is unset because for the other types or with an explicitly configured PID file, the main PID is always known\&. The guessing algorithm might come to incorrect conclusions if a daemon consists of more than one process\&. If the main PID cannot be determined, failure detection and automatic restarting of a service will not work reliably\&. Defaults to
494-\fByes\fR\&.
495-.RE
496-.PP
497-\fIPIDFile=\fR
498-.RS 4
499-Takes a path referring to the PID file of the service\&. Usage of this option is recommended for services where
500-\fIType=\fR
501-is set to
502-\fBforking\fR\&. The path specified typically points to a file below
503-/run/\&. If a relative path is specified it is hence prefixed with
504-/run/\&. The service manager will read the PID of the main process of the service from this file after start\-up of the service\&. The service manager will not write to the file configured here, although it will remove the file after the service has shut down if it still exists\&. The PID file does not need to be owned by a privileged user, but if it is owned by an unprivileged user additional safety restrictions are enforced: the file may not be a symlink to a file owned by a different user (neither directly nor indirectly), and the PID file must refer to a process already belonging to the service\&.
505-.sp
506-Note that PID files should be avoided in modern projects\&. Use
507-\fBType=notify\fR,
508-\fBType=notify\-reload\fR
509-or
510-\fBType=simple\fR
511-where possible, which does not require use of PID files to determine the main process of a service and avoids needless forking\&.
512-.RE
513-.PP

1737-T{
1738-":"
1739-T}:T{
1740-If the executable path is prefixed with ":", environment variable substitution (as described by the "Command Lines" section below) is not applied\&.
1741-T}
1742-T{
1743-"+"
1744-T}:T{
1745:If the executable path is prefixed with "+" then the process is executed with full privileges\&. In this mode privilege restrictions configured with \fIUser=\fR, \fIGroup=\fR, \fICapabilityBoundingSet=\fR or the various file system namespacing options (such as \fIPrivateDevices=\fR, \fIPrivateTmp=\fR) are not applied to the invoked command line (but still affect any other \fIExecStart=\fR, \fIExecStop=\fR, \&... lines)\&. However, note that this will not bypass options that apply to the whole control group, such as \fIDevicePolicy=\fR, see \fBsystemd.resource-control\fR(5) for the full list\&.
1746-T}
1747-T{
1748-"!"
1749-T}:T{
1750-Similar to the "+" character discussed above this permits invoking command lines with elevated privileges\&. However, unlike "+" the "!" character exclusively alters the effect of \fIUser=\fR, \fIGroup=\fR and \fISupplementaryGroups=\fR, i\&.e\&. only the stanzas that affect user and group credentials\&. Note that this setting may be combined with \fIDynamicUser=\fR, in which case a dynamic user/group pair is allocated before the command is invoked, but credential changing is left to the executed process itself\&.
1751-T}
1752-T{
1753-"!!"
1754-T}:T{
1755:This prefix is very similar to "!", however it only has an effect on systems lacking support for ambient process capabilities, i\&.e\&. without support for \fIAmbientCapabilities=\fR\&. It\*(Aqs intended to be used for unit files that take benefit of ambient capabilities to run processes with minimal privileges wherever possible while remaining compatible with systems that lack ambient capabilities support\&. Note that when "!!" is used, and a system lacking ambient capability support is detected any configured \fISystemCallFilter=\fR and \fICapabilityBoundingSet=\fR stanzas are implicitly modified, in order to permit spawned processes to drop credentials and capabilities themselves, even if this is configured to not be allowed\&. Moreover, if this prefix is used and a system lacking ambient capability support is detected \fIAmbientCapabilities=\fR will be skipped and not be applied\&. On systems supporting ambient capabilities, "!!" has no effect and is redundant\&.
1756-T}

   438	        # minimal; new services should use an rpc user + HMAC instead (audit L-10).
   439	        # Use an explicit 0640 rather than `chmod g=r`: the latter leaves the
   440	        # other-read bit at whatever bitcoind created (its umask), so the cookie's
   441	        # world-readability would hinge on the app's file mode. 0640 clears it.
   442	        chmod 0640 '${cfg.dataDir}/${optionalString cfg.regtest "regtest/"}.cookie'
   443	      '' + (optionalString cfg.regtest) ''
   444	        chmod g=x '${cfg.dataDir}/regtest'
   445	      '';
   446	
   447	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   448	        Type = "notify";
   449	        NotifyAccess = "all";
   450	        User = cfg.user;
   451	        Group = cfg.group;
   452	        TimeoutStartSec = "30min";
   453	        TimeoutStopSec = "30min";
   454	        ExecStart = "${cfg.package}/bin/bitcoind -datadir='${cfg.dataDir}'";
   455	        Restart = "on-failure";
   456	        UMask = mkIf cfg.dataDirReadableByGroup "0027";
   457	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.dataDir ];
   458	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce
   459	        // optionalAttrs zmqServerEnabled nbLib.allowNetlink;
   460	    };
   461	
   462	    users.users.${cfg.user} = {
   463	      isSystemUser = true;
   464	      group = cfg.group;
   465	    };
   466	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   467	    users.groups.bitcoinrpc-public = {};
   468

Usage: mv [OPTION]... [-T] SOURCE DEST
  or:  mv [OPTION]... SOURCE... DIRECTORY
  or:  mv [OPTION]... -t DIRECTORY SOURCE...
Rename SOURCE to DEST, or move SOURCE(s) to DIRECTORY.

Mandatory arguments to long options are mandatory for short options too.
      --backup[=CONTROL]       make a backup of each existing destination file
  -b                           like --backup but does not accept an argument
      --debug                  explain how a file is copied.  Implies -v
  -f, --force                  do not prompt before overwriting
  -i, --interactive            prompt before overwrite
  -n, --no-clobber             do not overwrite an existing file
If you specify more than one of -i, -f, -n, only the final one takes effect.
      --no-copy                do not copy if renaming fails
      --strip-trailing-slashes  remove any trailing slashes from each SOURCE
                                 argument
  -S, --suffix=SUFFIX          override the usual backup suffix
  -t, --target-directory=DIRECTORY  move all SOURCE arguments into DIRECTORY
  -T, --no-target-directory    treat DEST as a normal file
  --update[=UPDATE]            control which existing files are updated;
                                 UPDATE={all,none,older(default)}.  See below
  -u                           equivalent to --update[=older]
  -v, --verbose                explain what is being done
  -Z, --context                set SELinux security context of destination
                                 file to default type
      --help        display this help and exit
      --version     output version information and exit

UPDATE controls which existing files in the destination are replaced.
'all' is the default operation when an --update option is not specified,
and results in all existing files in the destination being replaced.
'none' is similar to the --no-clobber option, in that no files in the
destination are replaced, but also skipped files do not induce a failure.
'older' is the default operation when --update is specified, and results
in files being replaced if they're older than the corresponding source file.

1-nix-instantiate(1)          General Commands Manual         nix-instantiate(1)
2-
3-Name
4-       nix-instantiate - instantiate store derivations from Nix expressions
5-
6-Synopsis
7:       nix-instantiate [--parse | --eval [--strict] [--raw | --json | --xml] ]
8:       [--read-write-mode]  [--arg name value] [{--attr| -A} attrPath] [--add-
9-       root path] [--expr | -E] files…
10-
11-       nix-instantiate --find-file files…
12-
13-Description
14-       The command nix-instantiate  produces  store  derivations  from  (high-
15-       level)  Nix  expressions.   It evaluates the Nix expressions in each of
16-       files (which defaults  to  ./default.nix).  Each  top-level  expression
17-       should evaluate to a derivation, a list of derivations, or a set of de‐
18-       rivations.  The paths of the resulting store derivations are printed on
19-       standard output.
20-
--
28-
29-       •  --parse
30-
31-          Just parse the input files, and print their abstract syntax trees on
32-          standard output as a Nix expression.
33-
34:       •  --eval
35-
36-          Just  parse  and  evaluate  the input files, and print the resulting
37-          values on standard output.  Store derivations are not serialized and
38-          written to the store, but instead just hashed and discarded.
39-
40-                 Warning
41-
42-                 This option produces output which can be parsed as a Nix  ex‐
43-                 pression which will produce a different result than the input
44-                 expression  when  evaluated.   For example, these two Nix ex‐
45-                 pressions print the  same  result  despite  having  different
46-                 meaning:
47-
48:          $ nix-instantiate --eval --expr '{ a = {}; }'
49-          { a = <CODE>; }
50:          $ nix-instantiate --eval --expr '{ a = <CODE>; }'
51-          { a = <CODE>; }
52-
53-                 For  human-readable  output,  nix eval (experimental) is more
54-                 informative:
55-
56:          $ nix-instantiate --eval --expr 'a: a'
57-          <LAMBDA>
58-          $ nix eval --expr 'a: a'
59-          «lambda @ «string»:1:1»
60-
61-                 For machine-readable output, the --xml option produces  unam‐
62-                 biguous output:
63-
64:          $ nix-instantiate --eval --xml --expr '{ foo = <CODE>; }'
65-          <?xml version='1.0' encoding='utf-8'?>
66-          <expr>
67-            <attrs>
68-              <attr column="3" line="1" name="foo">
69-                <unevaluated />
70-              </attr>
71-            </attrs>
72-          </expr>
73-
74-       •  --find-file
75-
76-          Look  up  the  given files in Nix’s search path (as specified by the
--
78-          absolute paths on standard output. For instance, if NIX_PATH is nix‐
79-          pkgs=/home/alice/nixpkgs,  then  nix-instantiate  --find-file  nixp‐
80-          kgs/default.nix will print /home/alice/nixpkgs/default.nix.
81-
82-       •  --strict
83-
84:          When used with --eval, recursively evaluate list  elements  and  at‐
85-          tributes. Normally, such sub-expressions are left unevaluated (since
86-          the Nix language is lazy).
87-
88-                 Warning
89-
90-                 This  option  can  cause  non-termination,  because lazy data
91-                 structures can be infinitely large.
92-
93-       •  --raw
94-
95:          When used with --eval, the evaluation result must be a string, which
96-          is printed verbatim, without quoting, escaping or trailing newline.
97-
98-       •  --json
99-
100:          When used with --eval, print the resulting value as an  JSON  repre‐
101-          sentation  of  the abstract syntax tree rather than as a Nix expres‐
102-          sion.
103-
104-       •  --xml
105-
106:          When used with --eval, print the resulting value as an XML represen‐
107-          tation of the abstract syntax tree rather than as a Nix  expression.
108-          The schema is the same as that used by the toXML built-in.
109-
110:       •  --read-write-mode
111-
112:          When  used with --eval, perform evaluation in read/write mode so nix
113-          language features that require it will still work (at  the  cost  of
114-          needing  to do instantiation of every evaluated derivation). If this
115-          option is not enabled, there may be uninstantiated  store  paths  in
116-          the final output.
117-
118-Common Options
119-       Most Nix commands accept the following command-line options:
120-
121-       •  --help
122-
123-          Prints out a summary of the command syntax and exits.
124-

335-          of  the fourth element of the array in the foo attribute of the top-
336-          level expression.
337-
338:       •  --eval-store store-url

    49	# vulnix: 0 = clean, 2 = findings, 1 = scan error (NVD fetch, whitelist parse).
    50	# rc=2 is ALSO click's usage-error exit code, so additionally require the output
    51	# to be a JSON array — otherwise an error would read as a clean scan and the
    52	# workflow would close the tracking issue on garbage.
    53	if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && [ "$rc" -ne 2 ]; then
    54	  echo "vulnix scan FAILED (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1
    55	fi
    56	jq -e 'type == "array"' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    57	  || { echo "vulnix produced no valid JSON (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    58	
    59	# Summarize with the runner's native jq (present on GitHub runners and pop-os).
    60	# vulnix --json fields: `affected_by` holds only the ACTIVE (non-whitelisted)
    61	# CVEs — whitelist-masked ones are moved to the `whitelisted` array, and
    62	# derivations whose CVEs are ALL masked are omitted from the JSON entirely.
    63	# So filter on affected_by alone: also requiring `whitelisted` to be empty
    64	# silently dropped any derivation carrying BOTH a scoped-whitelisted collision
    65	# CVE and a genuinely new one — defeating the per-CVE scoping policy.
    66	real='[.[] | select(((.affected_by // []) | length) > 0)]'
    67	maxcvss='(([.cvssv3_basescore // {} | .[]] | max) // 0)'
    68	count=$(jq "$real | length" vulnix-report.json 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
    69	{
    70	  echo "# CVE scan — $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ)"
    71	  echo
    72	  echo "Scanned: representative node toplevel (bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver + nbxplorer), runtime closure"
    73	  echo "Affected store paths with non-whitelisted CVEs: **$count**"
    74	  echo
    75	  if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then
    76	    # shellcheck disable=SC2016  # jq program, not shell — \(...) is jq syntax
    77	    jq -r "$real | map(. + {_m: $maxcvss}) | sort_by(-._m) | .[] | \"## \(.pname // .name) \(.version // \"\") — CVSS \(._m)\n- \((.affected_by // []) | join(\", \"))\"" \
    78	      vulnix-report.json
    79	    echo
    80	    echo "Triage: fix in \`pkgs/overrides.nix\` (version-guarded); whitelist test/build"
    81	    echo "tooling (this scans the full test closure) or N/A CVEs in \`.github/cve-whitelist.toml\`."
    82	  else
    83	    echo "No action needed."
    84	  fi
    85	} > cve-summary.md
    86	
    87	# Emit ONLY the machine-readable result to $GITHUB_OUTPUT (logs go to stdout).
    88	if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then found=true; else found=false; fi
    89	echo "found=$found" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT:-/dev/stdout}"
    90	echo "scan complete: found=$found"
